\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}
and
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}
are independent of the forms
of the distributions
P_{X}
and
P_{Y}
.
To evaluate the performance of the tests
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}
and
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}
,
for any probability distribution
Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})
, we introduce the state as

\displaystyle\rho^{XY\mathcal{B}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathcal{X}\\
y\in\mathcal{Y}\end{subarray}}P_{X}(x)\overset{X}{|x\rangle\langle x|}\otimes P_{Y}(y)\overset{Y}{|y\rangle\langle y|}\otimes\rho_{Q,x,y}^{\mathcal{B}}.

(59)

where
\rho_{Q,x,y}^{\mathcal{B}}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}Q(s)\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{x,y,s}
. Then,
we introduce the mutual-information-type quantities;

\displaystyle I[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}

\displaystyle:=D(\rho^{XY\mathcal{B}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}||\rho^{Y}_{P_{Y}}\otimes\rho^{X\mathcal{B}}_{P_{X},Q}),

(60)

\displaystyle I[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}

\displaystyle:=D(\rho^{XY\mathcal{B}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}||\rho^{X}_{P_{X}}\otimes\rho^{Y\mathcal{B}}_{P_{Y},Q}),

(61)

\displaystyle I[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}

\displaystyle:=D(\rho^{XY\mathcal{B}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}||\rho^{X}_{P_{X}}\otimes\rho^{Y}_{P_{Y}}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{Q}),

(62)

where

\rho^{X\mathcal{B}}_{P_{X},Q}:=\mathrm{Tr}_{Y}[\rho^{XY\mathcal{B}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}],\rho^{Y\mathcal{B}}_{P_{Y},Q}:=\mathrm{Tr}_{X}[\rho^{XY\mathcal{B}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}]\text{ and }\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{Q}:=\mathrm{Tr}_{XY}[\rho^{XY\mathcal{B}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}].

To characterize the above quantities, we introduce
Rényi-type quantities;

\displaystyle I_{1-t}[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}:=-\frac{1-t}{t}\log\left(\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}P_{X}(x)\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}P_{Y}(y)\left({\rho_{Q,x,y}^{\mathcal{B}}}\right)^{1-t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-t}}\right]\right),

(63)

\displaystyle I_{1-t}[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}:=-\frac{1-t}{t}\log\left(\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}P_{Y}(y)\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}P_{X}(x)\left({\rho_{Q,x,y}^{\mathcal{B}}}\right)^{1-t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-t}}\right]\right),

(64)

\displaystyle I_{1-t}[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}:=-\frac{1-t}{t}\log\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathcal{X}\\
y\in\mathcal{Y}\end{subarray}}P_{X}(x)P_{Y}(y)\left({\rho_{Q,x,y}^{\mathcal{B}}}\right)^{1-t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-t}}\right],

(65)

where
\rho_{Q,x,y}^{\mathcal{B}}:=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}Q(s)\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}
.
By using the above quantities, the mutual-information-type quantities are recovered as

\displaystyle I[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\to 0}I_{1-t}[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q},

(66)

\displaystyle I[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\to 0}I_{1-t}[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q},

(67)

\displaystyle I[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\to 0}I_{1-t}[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}.

(68)

The performance of the tests
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}
and
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}

are evaluated by the following lemma.

Lemma 5
.

Assume that
the subset
\left\{\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}:x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y},s\in\mathcal{S}\right\}\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B})
satisfies
Assumption
1
.
For any real number
\varepsilon>0
,
any element
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
,
any subsets
\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}}\}
,
\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\}
, and
\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}
,
the tests
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}
and
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}
satisfy the following,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\right)\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]\leq f(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|,t)(1+\varepsilon)^{n}\left(2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{1}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 170.0pt+\left.2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)}\right),

(69)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}\right)\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]\leq f(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|,t)(1+\varepsilon)^{n}\left(2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{2}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 170.0pt+\left.2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)}\right),

(70)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\right)\right]\leq g_{2}(n,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n\widehat{R}_{1}},

(71)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}\right)\right]\leq g_{3}(n,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2})},

(72)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}}\right)\right]\leq g_{1}(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n\widehat{R}_{2}},

(73)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}\right)\right]\leq g_{3}(n,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2})},

(74)

where
\widehat{R}_{1},\widehat{R}_{2}>0
,
t\in(0,1)
, and the symbols used here are defined as

\displaystyle f(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|,t):=(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1+\frac{t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}{2}+(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|},\quad g_{1}(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|):=n^{\frac{\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{B}\right|(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)}{2}}\left|\Lambda^{n}_{\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}\right|^{\left|\mathcal{X}\right|},

(75)

\displaystyle g_{2}(n,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|):=n^{\frac{\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|\left|\mathcal{B}\right|(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)}{2}}\left|\Lambda^{n}_{\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}\right|^{\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|}\hskip 2.0pt,\quad g_{3}(n,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|):=n^{\frac{\left|\mathcal{B}\right|(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)}{2}}\left|\Lambda^{n}_{\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}\right|.\hskip 48.0pt

(76)

Therefore, when
\widehat{R}_{1},\widehat{R}_{2}
satisfy the conditions

\displaystyle\widehat{R}_{1}

\displaystyle<\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q},

(77)

\displaystyle\widehat{R}_{2}

\displaystyle<\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q},

(78)

\displaystyle\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2}

\displaystyle<\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q},

(79)

the tests
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}
and
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}
well detect
the hypotheses
H_{1}^{X}
and
H_{1}^{Y}
, respectively for large enough
n
.
This lemma is shown in Subsection
VI-C
.
A corollary of Lemma
5
is mentioned below, which will be required to give a proof of achievability for deriving the capacity region for CQ-AVMAC (see Theorem
3
).

Corollary 1
.

Assume that
the subset
\left\{\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}:x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y},s\in\mathcal{S}\right\}\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B})
satisfies Assumption
1
.
For any real number
\epsilon>0
and
any element
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
,
the tests
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}
and
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}
satisfy the following,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\right)\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]\leq f(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|,t)(1+\varepsilon)^{n}\left(2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{1}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 170.0pt+\left.2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)}\right)

(80)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}\right)\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]\leq f(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|,t)(1+\varepsilon)^{n}\left(2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{2}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 170.0pt+\left.2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)}\right)

(81)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n},s^{n}}\right)\right]\leq g_{2}(n,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n\widehat{R}_{1}},

(82)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{s^{n}}\right)\right]\leq g_{3}(n,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2})},

(83)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},s^{n}}\right)\right]\leq g_{1}(n,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n\widehat{R}_{2}},

(84)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{s^{n}}\right)\right]\leq g_{3}(n,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2})},

(85)

where
\widehat{R}_{1},\widehat{R}_{2}>0
,
t\in(0,1)
.
For each
x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n},y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n},s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
, we define the following

\displaystyle\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},s^{n}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}}P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n},s^{n}},

(86)

\displaystyle\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n},s^{n}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n},s^{n}},

(87)

\displaystyle\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{s^{n}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n},s^{n}}.

(88)

VI-C

Proof of Lemma
5

We note from Lemma
2
, for any
x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n},y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}
, the states
\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n}},\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}
and
\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{U,n}
commute with each other. Thus, the tests
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}
and
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}
commute with each other. Using similar arguments, we see that the tests
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}
and
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X}
commute with each other as well.

Further, the states
\widehat{\rho}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{U,P_{X},P_{Y}},{\rho^{\mathcal{H}_{X}^{\otimes n}}_{P_{X}}}\otimes\rho^{Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{U,P_{Y}},{\rho^{\mathcal{H}_{Y}^{\otimes n}}_{P_{Y}}}\otimes\rho^{X^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{U,P_{X}}
and
\rho^{\mathcal{H}_{X}^{\otimes n}}_{P_{X}}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{H}_{Y}^{\otimes n}}_{P_{Y}}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{U,n}
are permutation invariant,
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}
and
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}
are permutation invariant. Thus, for any permutation
\pi\in S_{n}
, the following holds,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\right)\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\right)\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},\pi(s^{n})}\right],

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}\right)\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}\right)\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},\pi(s^{n})}\right].

Thus, for any
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{n}
, we upper-bound the quantity
\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\right)\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]
as follows,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\right)\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{a}{=}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}^{X}_{n}\right)\frac{1}{\left|T_{Q_{s^{n}}}\right|}\sum_{\pi\in S_{n}}\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},\pi(s^{n})}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\right)\frac{1}{\left|T_{Q_{s^{n}}}\right|}\sum_{\widehat{s}^{n}\in T_{Q_{s^{n}}}}\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},\widehat{s}^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{b}{\leq}(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\right)\sum_{\widehat{s}^{n}\in T_{Q_{s^{n}}}}Q^{n}_{s^{n}}(\widehat{s}^{n})\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},\widehat{s}^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\right)\sum_{\widehat{s}^{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{n}}Q^{n}_{s^{n}}(\widehat{s}^{n})\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},\widehat{s}^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{c}{=}(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\right)\left(\rho^{{VXY\mathcal{B}}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q_{s^{n}}}\right)^{\otimes n}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}\right)\left(\rho^{{XY\mathcal{B}}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q_{s^{n}}}\right)^{\otimes n}\right]+(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}\right)\left(\rho^{{XY\mathcal{B}}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q_{s^{n}}}\right)^{\otimes n}\right],

(89)

where
a
follows from the fact that
\hat{\mathbb{T}}^{X}_{n}
is permutation invariant,
b
follows from (
28
),
c
follows from (
59
). We upper bound the first term in (
89
) as follows

\displaystyle\hskip 14.0pt(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}\right)\left(\rho^{{XY\mathcal{B}}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q_{s^{n}}}\right)^{\otimes n}\right]

\displaystyle=(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{Q_{s^{n}},x_{i},y_{i}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{a}{=}(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1}\sum_{y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}}P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\mathrm{Tr}\left[\sum_{x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{Q_{s^{n}},x_{i},y_{i}}\right)\left\{\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n}}\prec 2^{n\widehat{R}_{1}}\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{b}{\leq}(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1}2^{nt\widehat{R}_{1}}\sum_{y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}}P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\mathrm{Tr}\left[\sum_{x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{Q_{s^{n}},x_{i},y_{i}}\right)(\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n}})^{-t}(\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}})^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{c}{\leq}(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1}2^{nt\widehat{R}_{1}}n^{\frac{t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}{2}}\left|\Lambda^{n}_{\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}\right|^{t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|}\sum_{y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}}P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\mathrm{Tr}\left[\sum_{x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{Q_{s^{n}},x_{i},y_{i}}\right)^{1-t}(\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}})^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{d}{\leq}(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1+\frac{t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}{2}+(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|}2^{nt\widehat{R}_{1}}\sum_{y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}}P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\mathrm{Tr}\left[\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}P_{X}(x)\left(\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{Q_{s^{n}},x,y_{i}}\right)^{1-t}\right)(\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}})^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{e}{\leq}(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1+\frac{t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}{2}+(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|}2^{nt\widehat{R}_{1}}\sum_{y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}}P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\left(\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}P_{X}(x)\left(\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{Q_{s^{n}},x,y_{i}}\right)^{1-t}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{1-t}}\right]\right)^{1-t}

\displaystyle=(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1+\frac{t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}{2}+(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|}2^{nt\widehat{R}_{1}}\sum_{y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}}P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}P_{X}(x)\left(\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{Q_{s^{n}},x,y_{i}}\right)^{1-t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-t}}\right]\right)^{1-t}

\displaystyle=(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1+\frac{t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}{2}+(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|}2^{nt\widehat{R}_{1}}\left(\sum_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}P_{Y}(y)\left(\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\left(\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}}P_{X}(x)\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{Q_{s^{n}},x,y}\right)^{1-t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-t}}\right]\right)^{1-t}\right)^{n}

\displaystyle\overset{f}{=}(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1+\frac{t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}{2}+(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|}2^{nt(\widehat{R}_{1}-I_{1-t}[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q_{s^{n}}})}

\displaystyle{\leq}(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1+\frac{t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}{2}+(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)t\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|}2^{nt(\widehat{R}_{1}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q})}

\displaystyle{\leq}f(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|,t)2^{nt(\widehat{R}_{1}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q})},

(90)

where
a
follows from the definition of
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}
in (
56
),
b
follows from the fact that since
\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n}}
commutes with
\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}
(which follows from Lemma
2
), we have
\left\{\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n}}-2^{n\widehat{R}_{1}}\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\prec 0\right\}\leq 2^{nt\widehat{R}_{1}}\left(\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n}}\right)^{-t}\left(\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\right)^{t}
for all
t\in(0,1)
,
c
follows using arguments similar to that used in the proof of Lemma
3
,
d
follows from Fact
2
,
e
follows from (
15
) of Fact
7
and
f
follows from (
63
).

Similarly, we can upper-bound the second term in (
89
) as follows

\displaystyle\hskip 14.0pt(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|-1}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}\right)\left(\rho^{{XY\mathcal{B}}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q_{s^{n}}}\right)^{\otimes n}\right]\leq f(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|,t)2^{nt(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q})}.

(91)

Thus, for any
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}^{n}
,
from
eqs.

90
,
91
and
89
, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\right)\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]\leq f(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|,t)\left(2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{1}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 170.0pt+\left.2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)}\right).

(92)

The combination of (
2
) and (
92
) implies (
69
)
for any
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}
.

Using similar calculations we can prove (
70
) for the test
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}
as well. Now, for any collection
\left\{\sigma_{y^{n}}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes n})
, we have the following upper-bound:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{a}{=}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\frac{1}{\left|S_{y^{n}}\right|}\sum_{\pi\in S_{n,y^{n}}}V^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}(\pi)(\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}){V^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}}^{\dagger}(\pi)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{b}{\leq}n^{\frac{\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|\left|\mathcal{B}\right|(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)}{2}}\left|\Lambda^{n}_{\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}\right|^{\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{c}{\leq}n^{\frac{\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|\left|\mathcal{B}\right|(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)}{2}}\left|\Lambda^{n}_{\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}\right|^{\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|}2^{-n\widehat{R}_{1}}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}\widehat{\rho}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{U,P_{X},P_{Y}}\right]\leq n^{\frac{\left|\mathcal{X}\right|\left|\mathcal{B}\right|(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)}{2}}\left|\Lambda^{n}_{\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}\right|^{\left|\mathcal{X}\right|}2^{-n\widehat{R}_{1}}.

Here, to show
a
, we define the subgroup
S_{n,y^{n}}:=\left\{\pi\in S_{n}:\pi(y^{n})=(y^{n})\right\}
.
Then, since
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}
is permutation invariant,
any
\pi\in S_{n,y^{n}}
satisfies the relation

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle{=}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes V^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}(\pi)(\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}){V^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}}^{\dagger}(\pi)\right)\right],

which implies the equality
a
.
Further, the inequality
b
above, follows from Lemma
1
and
c
follows from the definition of
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}
, given in (
56
). Using similar approaches one can derive a proof for
eq.

72
.

The proofs for
eqs.

73
and
74
follow similarly. This completes the proof of Lemma
5
.

VI-D

Universal Simultaneous Test for Multiple Independence Testing

In the problem discussed in the above subsection, for each classical subsystem, we designed a test that accepts the states where the quantum sub-system is not correlated with this particular classical subsystem with arbitrarily small probability and accepts the states where the quantum subsystem is correlated with each of the classical subsystems with high probability. A drawback of this test is that it won’t be able to make any decision for the states where the quantum sub-system is not correlated with that particular classical subsystem. Therefore, we call these above tests as
non-simultaneous universal tests
.

We now aim to construct a test that accepts the states where the quantum subsystem is independent of any of the classical subsystems with arbitrarily low probability and accepts the state where the quantum subsystem is correlated with all the classical subsystems with high probability.
For simplicity, we assume that

|\mathcal{S}|<\infty
.

For each
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
and
\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}}\},\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\}\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes n})
and
\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes n})
, we first consider the following probabilities

\displaystyle P_{0,s^{n}}:=\mathrm{Tr}\left[\mathbb{T}^{\star}_{n}\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]

(93)

\displaystyle P_{1,Y,\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}}\}}:=\mathrm{Tr}\left[\mathbb{T}^{\star}_{n}\left({\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}}}\right)\right],

(94)

\displaystyle P_{1,X,\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\}}:=\mathrm{Tr}\left[\mathbb{T}^{\star}_{n}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\right)\right],

(95)

\displaystyle P_{1,\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}}:=\mathrm{Tr}\left[\mathbb{T}^{\star}_{n}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}\right)\right].

(96)

Our aim here is to design a measurement-based test
\mathbb{T}^{\star}_{n}
in the composite Hilbert space

\mathcal{H}_{X}^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{Y}^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes n}
such that for each
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
,

P_{0,s^{n}}
is arbitrarily high and for each
\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}}\},\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\}\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes n})
and
\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes n})
, the probabilities

P_{1,Y,\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}}\}},P_{1,X,\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\}}
and
P_{1,\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}}
are arbitrarily small.

This problem is very similar to the problem discussed by Sen in
[
42
]
, in the context of CQ-MAC (classical-quantum multiple-access channel). However, we note here that there is a subtle difference between the problem discussed above and the problem studied in
[
42
]
. To highlight this difference we make the following remark:

Remark 1
.

In
[
42
]
, the author considered the singleton collections
H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{0},H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{1,Y},H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{1,X},H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{1}
which are defined as follows,

\displaystyle H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{0}

\displaystyle:=\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y}},

\displaystyle H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{1,Y}

\displaystyle:=\rho^{Y^{n}}_{P_{Y}}\otimes\rho^{X^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X}},

\displaystyle H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{1,X}

\displaystyle:=\rho^{X^{n}}_{P_{X}}\otimes\rho^{Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{Y}},

\displaystyle H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\rho^{X^{n}}_{P_{X}}\otimes\rho^{Y^{n}}_{P_{Y}}\otimes\rho^{Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}},

where

\displaystyle\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n}},

\displaystyle\rho^{X^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{Y}}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{Tr}_{Y^{n}}\left[\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y}}\right],\rho^{X^{n}}_{P_{X}}:=\mathrm{Tr}_{Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}\left[\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y}}\right],

\displaystyle\rho^{Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{Y}}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{Tr}_{X^{n}}\left[\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y}}\right],\rho^{Y^{n}}_{P_{Y}}:=\mathrm{Tr}_{X^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}\left[\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y}}\right].

Here the aim is to find a hypothesis test-based measurement that accepts
H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{0}
with very high probability and accepts
H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{1,X},H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{1,Y}
and
H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{1}
with arbitrarily low probability. Unlike
H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{0}
, which has only a single state, in
H_{0}
, mentioned in (
48
), not only do we have more than one state, but also we have a dependency of the index
s^{n}
i.e. for each
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
, the state changes. Similarly, each of
H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{1,X},H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{1,Y}
and
H^{\mbox{Sen}}_{1}
has only a single state and that too is one of the correct marginals of
\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y}}
. However, the collections
H_{1,X},H_{1,Y}
and
H_{1}
respectively mentioned in
eqs.

49
,
50
and
51
, can be arbitrarily large and may not even be the correct marginals of the states in
H_{0}
, mentioned in (
48
).

Despite these subtle differences as mentioned in Remark
1
,
we solve the above problem using the projector designed in the proof of Theorem
1
, along with the techniques developed in
[
42
]
, by increasing the dimension of the composite Hilbert space in a controlled way and mapping the original collection of states
\left\{\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right\}_{s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}
and
\left\{\sigma^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n}}\}}\right\}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n}}\}\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes n})\end{subarray}}
inside the enlarged space using an inclusion isometric map:

\mathcal{E}^{\star}:\mathcal{H}_{X}^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{Y}^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes n}\xhookrightarrow{}\mathcal{H}_{X^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{Y^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}},

because
\mathcal{H}_{X^{\prime}},\mathcal{H}_{Y^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
are enlarged Hilbert spaces containing
\mathcal{H}_{X}^{\otimes n},\mathcal{H}_{Y}^{\otimes n}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes n}
respectively.
Using the isometry
\mathcal{E}^{\star}(\cdot)

and
a projector
{\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n}
in the enlarged composite Hilbert space

\mathcal{H}_{X^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{Y^{\prime}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
,
we define
the probabilities below

\displaystyle\widehat{P}_{0,s^{n}}({\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{Tr}\left[{\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n}\mathcal{E}^{\star}\left(\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right)\right],

(97)

\displaystyle\widehat{P}_{1,Y,\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}}\}}({\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{Tr}\left[{\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n}\left(\mathrm{Tr}_{X^{\prime}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\left[\mathcal{E}^{\star}\left(\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right)\right]\otimes\mathrm{Tr}_{Y^{\prime}}\left[\mathcal{E}^{\star}\left(\sigma^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n}}\}}\right)\right]\right)\right],

(98)

\displaystyle\widehat{P}_{1,X,\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\}}({\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{Tr}\left[{\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n}\left(\mathrm{Tr}_{Y^{\prime}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\left[\mathcal{E}^{\star}\left(\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right)\right]\otimes\mathrm{Tr}_{X^{\prime}}\bigg[\mathcal{E}^{\star}\bigg(\sigma^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n}}\}}\bigg)\bigg]\right)\right],

(99)

\displaystyle\widehat{P}_{1,\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}}({\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{Tr}\left[{\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n}\left(\mathrm{Tr}_{Y^{\prime}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\left[\mathcal{E}^{\star}\left(\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right)\right]\otimes\mathrm{Tr}_{X^{\prime}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\left[\mathcal{E}^{\star}\left(\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right)\right]\otimes\mathrm{Tr}_{X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}}\left[\mathcal{E}^{\star}\left(\sigma^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n}}\}}\right)\right]\right)\right].

(100)

Then, we have the following lemma, which is useful for generalized independent testing.

Lemma 6
.

For
t\in(0,1)
, let the pair (
\widehat{R}_{1},\widehat{R}_{2}
) satisfy the following conditions

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle<\widehat{R}_{1}<\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I_{1-t}[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q},

(101)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle<\widehat{R}_{2}<\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I_{1-t}[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q},

(102)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle<\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2}<\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I_{1-t}[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q},

(103)

where
I_{1-t}[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q},I_{1-t}[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}
and
I_{1-t}[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}
are defined in
eqs.

63
,
64
and
65
respectively.
Then, for any positive integer
n
, there exists a POVM
{\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n}
to satisfy the following conditions.
(i) The relations

\displaystyle\forall\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}}\}\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes n})

\displaystyle,\widehat{P}_{1,Y,\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}}\}}({\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n})\leq g_{1}(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n\widehat{R}_{2}+1},

(104)

\displaystyle\forall\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\}\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes n})

\displaystyle,\widehat{P}_{1,X,\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\}}({\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n})\leq g_{2}(n,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n\widehat{R}_{2}+1},

(105)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes n})

\displaystyle,\widehat{P}_{1,\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}}({\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n})\leq g_{3}(n,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2})+1}

(106)

hold, where

g_{1}(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|),g_{2}(n,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)
and
g_{3}(n,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)
are defined in
eqs.

75
and
76
respectively.
(ii) In addition, for any arbitrary
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists a sufficiently
large
n_{0}
such that the relation

\displaystyle\forall s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}

\displaystyle,\widehat{P}_{0,s^{n}}({\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n})\geq 1-76\delta

(107)

holds for any integer
n\geq n_{0}
.

Proof.

See Appendix
C
for the proof.

We need the following lemma to prove Lemma
6
.

Lemma 7
.

There exist projectors
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X},\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}
and
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}
which,
\forall s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
,
\forall\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}}\},\forall\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\},
and
\forall\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}
satisfy the following

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X}\right)\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq f(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}\right|,t)2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{2}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I_{1-t}[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)},

(108)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X}\left({\rho^{\mathcal{H}_{Y}^{\otimes n}}_{P_{Y}}}\otimes\sigma^{X^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}}\}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq g_{1}(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n\widehat{R}_{2}},

(109)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}\right)\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq f(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}\right|,t)2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{1}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I_{1-t}[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)},

(110)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,Y}\left({\rho^{\mathcal{H}_{X}^{\otimes n}}_{P_{X}}}\otimes\sigma^{Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{Y},\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq g_{2}(n,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n\widehat{R}_{1}},

(111)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}\right)\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq f(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}\right|,t)2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I_{1-t}[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)},

(112)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}\left({\rho^{\mathcal{H}_{X}^{\otimes n}}_{P_{X}}}\otimes{\rho^{\mathcal{H}_{Y}^{\otimes n}}_{P_{Y}}}\otimes\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq g_{3}(n,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2})},

(113)

where
\widehat{R}_{1},\widehat{R}_{2}>0
,
t\in(0,1)
,
f(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}\right|,t)
,
g_{1}(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|),g_{2}(n,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)
,
g_{3}(n,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)
are defined in
eqs.

75
and
76
respectively and the states
\sigma^{X^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}}\}},\sigma^{Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{Y},\{\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{y^{n}}\}},\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}
are the respective marginals of the state mentioned in (
52
).

Proof.

See Appendix
D
for the proof.

Remark 2
.

Note that for the case of general
\mathcal{S}
, if
\left\{\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}:x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y},s\in\mathcal{S}\right\}
satisfies Assumption
1
, Lemma
6
can be directly extended for the case of general
\mathcal{S}
, using (
2
). However, we have omitted this calculation for simplicity.

We now mention a corollary of Lemma
6
, which will be required to give an alternative proof (see subsection
F
) of achievability for deriving the capacity of Classical-Quantum Arbitrarily Varying Channels (CQ-AVMAC) under randomization of codes.

Corollary 2
.

Consider the collection
\left\{\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right\}_{s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}
, where for each
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n},\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}
is defined in (
47
). Then, for any arbitrary
\delta\in(0,1)
and large enough
n
, the POVM
{\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n}
mentioned in (
236
) satisfies the following:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}-{\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n}\right)\rho^{X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 76\delta,

(114)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[{\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n}\left(\rho^{Y^{\prime}}_{P_{Y}}\otimes\rho^{X^{\prime}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{P_{X},s^{n}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq g_{1}(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n\widehat{R}_{2}+1},

(115)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[{\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n}\left(\rho^{X^{\prime}}_{P_{X}}\otimes\rho^{Y^{\prime}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{P_{Y},s^{n}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq g_{2}(n,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n\widehat{R}_{1}+1},

(116)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[{\mathbb{T}}^{\star}_{n}\left(\rho^{X^{\prime}}_{P_{X}}\otimes\rho^{Y^{\prime}}_{P_{Y}}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{s^{n}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq g_{3}(n,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2})+1},

(117)

where
(\widehat{R}_{1},\widehat{R}_{2})
satisfies
eqs.

101
,
102
and
103
, and for each
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
, we define the states as
\rho^{X^{\prime}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{P_{X},s^{n}},\rho^{Y^{\prime}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{P_{Y},s^{n}}
and
\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{s^{n}}
as follows,

\displaystyle\rho^{X^{\prime}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{P_{X},s^{n}}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{Tr}_{Y^{\prime}}\left[\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]=\frac{1}{\left|\mathcal{A}\right|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n},r\in[\left|\mathcal{A}\right|]\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{\prime}}{|x^{n},a_{r}\rangle\langle x^{n},a_{r}|}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{(x^{n},a_{r}),s^{n},\delta},

\displaystyle\rho^{Y^{\prime}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{P_{Y},s^{n}}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{Tr}_{X^{\prime}}\left[\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]=\frac{1}{\left|\mathcal{A}\right|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n},t\in[\left|\mathcal{A}\right|]\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{\prime}}{|y^{n},b_{t}\rangle\langle y^{n},b_{t}|}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{(y^{n},b_{t}),s^{n},\delta},

\displaystyle\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{s^{n}}

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{Tr}_{X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}}\left[\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]=\frac{1}{\left|\mathcal{A}\right|^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n},y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\\
r\in[\left|\mathcal{A}\right|],t\in[\left|\mathcal{A}\right|]\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{(x^{n},a_{r}),(y^{n},b_{t}),s^{n}\delta},

where for each
x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n},r\in[\left|\mathcal{A}\right|]
and
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n},t\in[\left|\mathcal{A}\right|]
we have,

\displaystyle\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{(x^{n},a_{r}),s^{n},\delta}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\left|\mathcal{A}\right|}\sum_{y^{n},t\in[\left|\mathcal{A}\right|]}P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{(x^{n},a_{r}),(y^{n},b_{t}),s^{n},\delta},

\displaystyle\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{(y^{n},b_{t}),\delta}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\left|\mathcal{A}\right|}\sum_{x^{n},r\in[\left|\mathcal{A}\right|]}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{(x^{n},a_{r}),(y^{n},b_{t}),s^{n},\delta},

and
\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}_{(x^{n},a_{r}),(y^{n},b_{t}),s^{n},\delta}
is defined in (
225
).

Proof.

See Appendix
E
for the proof.

Remark 3
.

In the above method of universal testing, we have a single test that accepts the states where the quantum subsystem is correlated with all the classical subsystems with high probability and rejects the states where the quantum sub-system is not correlated with any of the classical subsystems
simultanously
with arbitrarily high probability. This is the reason this test is called a
simultaneous universal test
.

VII
Reliable communication over Classical Quantum Arbitrarily Varying Multiple Access Channel (CQ-AVMAC)

VII-A

Case with
2
senders

First, we discuss classical quantum arbitrarily varying multiple access channels (CQ-AVMAC) for the case with
2
senders.
This case is modeled as follows.

Definition 16
.

(channel) We model a two-sender classical-quantum arbitrarily varying multiple-access channel (CQ-AVMAC) between parties
Alice (sender
1
), Bob (sender
2
) and Charlie (receiver) as a map

\mathcal{N}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\to B^{n}}_{s^{n}}:(x^{n},y^{n})\to\rho^{B^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n},s^{n}},

where
x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n},y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}
and
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
(where
\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|).
For each
x^{n}:=(x_{1}\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathcal{X}^{n},y^{n}:=(y_{1}\cdots,y_{n})\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}
and
s^{n}:=(s_{1}\cdots,s_{n})\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
,
we define the state
\rho^{B^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n},s^{n}}
to be
\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{B}_{x_{i},y_{i},s_{i}}
.
Further, Alice, Bob (the senders), and Charlie (the receiver) have no information about the sequence
s^{n}.

We aim to use this channel and enable Alice to transmit a message
m_{1}\in[2^{nR_{1}}]
and Bob to transmit a message
m_{2}\in[2^{nR_{2}}]
, such that Charlie can recover
(m_{1},m_{2})
with high probability for every
s^{n}
.

Definition 17
.

(Deterministic code)
An
(n,2^{nR_{1}},2^{nR_{2}})
-code
\mathcal{C}_{n}
for communication over a CQ-AVMAC consists of

•

two encoding functions
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{1}:\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\to\mathcal{X}^{n}
and
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{2}:\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\to\mathcal{Y}^{n}
where
\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}:=[2^{nR_{1}}]
and
\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}:=[2^{nR_{2}}]

•

a decoding POVM
\{\mathcal{D}_{m_{1},m_{2}}:(m_{1},m_{2})\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\times\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\}
.

An
(n,2^{nR_{1}},2^{nR_{2}})
-code
\mathcal{C}_{n}
for communication over a CQ-AVMAC is called
an
(n,2^{nR_{1}},2^{nR_{2}},\beta)
-code when for any
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
,
the average probability
\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})
of error
satisfies

\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n}):=\frac{1}{2^{nR_{1}}}\frac{1}{2^{nR_{2}}}\sum_{m_{1}=1}^{2^{nR_{1}}}\sum_{m_{2}=1}^{2^{nR_{2}}}e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})<\beta,

where
e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n}):=1-\mathrm{Tr}[D_{m_{1},m_{2}}\mathcal{N}^{X^{n}\to B^{n}}_{s^{n}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{1}(m_{1}),\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{2}(m_{2}))].

Definition 18
.

A pair
\left(R_{1},R_{2}\right)
is called an achievable deterministic rate-pair for a given CQ-AVMAC, if for any
\beta>0,\delta_{1}>0\text{ and }\delta_{2}>0
and sufficiently large
n
, there exists a
\left(n,2^{nR_{1}},2^{nR_{2}},\beta\right)
deterministic code
\mathcal{C}_{n}
such that

\begin{split}\frac{1}{n}\log|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|&>R_{1}-\delta_{1},\\
\frac{1}{n}\log|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|&>R_{2}-\delta_{2},\end{split}\begin{split}\hskip 20.0pt\sup_{s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})<\beta.\end{split}

The achievable deterministic code rate region
\mathcal{R}_{d}
(also known as deterministic code capacity region) is defined to be the closure of all possible achievable deterministic rate pairs.

Definition 19
.

(Random code)
An
(n,2^{nR_{1}},2^{nR_{2}})
random code
\mathcal{C}_{n}
for communication over a CQ-AVMAC consists of

•

two encoding functions
\mathcal{E}^{(n),\gamma_{1}}_{1}:\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\to\mathcal{X}^{n}
and
\mathcal{E}^{(n),\gamma_{2}}_{2}:\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\to\mathcal{Y}^{n}
, parameterized by two random variables
\gamma_{1}
,
\gamma_{2}
respectively, where
\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}:=[2^{nR_{1}}]
,
\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}:=[2^{nR_{2}}]
,

•

a decoding POVM
\{\mathcal{D}^{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}_{m_{1},m_{2}}:(m_{1},m_{2})\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\times\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\}
, parameterized by
\gamma_{1}
,
\gamma_{2}
.

In the above,
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\sim G_{1}\times G_{2}
are two independent random variables taking values over two finite sets.

An
(n,2^{nR_{1}},2^{nR_{2}})
random code
\mathcal{C}_{n}
for communication over a CQ-AVMAC is called
an
(n,2^{nR_{1}},2^{nR_{2}},\beta)
-code when for any
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
,
the average probability
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]
of error under the expectation of choices of random code (choices of
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
) satisfies

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]:=\frac{1}{2^{nR_{1}}}\frac{1}{2^{nR_{2}}}\sum_{m_{1}=1}^{2^{nR_{1}}}\sum_{m_{2}=1}^{2^{nR_{2}}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]<\beta,

where
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]:=1-\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma_{1}\sim G_{1}\\
\gamma_{2}\sim G_{2}\end{subarray}}\left[\mathrm{Tr}[D^{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}_{m_{1},m_{2}}\mathcal{N}^{X^{n}\to B^{n}}_{s^{n}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),\gamma_{1}}_{1}(m_{1}),\mathcal{E}^{(n),\gamma_{2}}_{2}(m_{2}))]\right].

When

\gamma_{1}
is shared between Alice (sender
1
) and Charlie (the receiver) and
\gamma_{2}
is shared between Bob (sender
2
) and Charlie (the receiver),
the above random code
\mathcal{C}_{n}

can be realized.

Definition 20
.

A pair
\left(R_{1},R_{2}\right)
is called an
achievable randomized rate-pair
for a given CQ-AVMAC, if for any
\beta>0,\delta_{1}>0\text{ and }\delta_{2}>0
and sufficiently large
n
, there exists a
\left(n,2^{nR_{1}},2^{nR_{2}},\beta\right)
random code
\mathcal{C}_{n}
such that

\begin{split}\frac{1}{n}\log|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|&>R_{1}-\delta_{1},\\
\frac{1}{n}\log|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|&>R_{2}-\delta_{2},\end{split}\begin{split}\hskip 20.0pt\sup_{s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}[\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})]<\beta.\end{split}

The achievable random code rate region
\mathcal{R}_{r}
(also known as random code capacity region) is defined to be the closure of all possible achievable randomized rate-pairs.

Since the input systems
X,Y
are classical we will consider them as a pair of random variables with support sets
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
. Given
X\sim P_{X}
and
Y\sim P_{Y}
with a joint probability distribution
P_{XY}:=P_{X}\cdot P_{Y}
, we define the following set

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{P_{X},P_{Y}}:=\left\{\forall(R_{1},R_{2}):\begin{array}[]{cc}0\leq R_{1}&\hskip-10.0pt\leq\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\\
0\leq R_{2}&\hskip-10.0pt\leq\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\\
R_{1}+R_{2}&\hskip-10.0pt\leq\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\end{array}\right\},

where
I[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q},I[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}
and
I[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}
are defined in
eqs.

61
,
60
and
62
.
Further, we define
\mathcal{R}^{\star}
as follows,

\mathcal{R}^{\star}:=conv\left(\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}P_{X}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{X})\\
P_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{Y})\end{subarray}}\mathcal{R}_{P_{X},P_{Y}}\right).

(121)

In the theorem below we establish the random code capacity region
\mathcal{R}_{r}
by giving two achievability proofs and a matching converse. The first achievability proof is based on the non-simultaneous test designed in Lemma
5
in subsection

VI-B
. The second achievability proof is based on the simultaneous test designed in Lemma
7
in subsection

VI-D
. The detailed proof for this alternate approach is given in Appendix
F
.
For both these proofs we obtain constraints on the pair
(R_{1},R_{2})
, by showing that for every
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
, the average probability of error averaged over the random choice of code goes to zero if these constraints are met.

Theorem
3
.

Assume that
the subset
\left\{\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}:x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y},s\in\mathcal{S}\right\}\subset\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B})
satisfies Assumption
1
.
The random code capacity region
\mathcal{R}_{r}
of a CQ-AVMAC is equal to
\mathcal{R}^{\star}
(See (
121
) for the definition).

This theorem is shown in subsections
IV-C
and
VII-C
. In subsection
IV-C
, we show the proof of the achievability part of Theorem
3
by designing a joint decoding strategy based on the non-simultaneous test designed in Lemma
5
in subsection

VI-B
.

VII-B

Achievability part of Theorem
3

Since

\mathcal{R}^{\star}
is given as convex full of

\mathcal{R}_{P_{X},P_{Y}}
with choices of
P_{X},P_{Y}
,
once
every rate-pair
(R_{1},R_{2})\in\mathcal{R}_{P_{X},P_{Y}}

is achieved with any pair
P_{X},P_{Y}
,
every rate-pair
(R_{1},R_{2})\in\mathcal{R}^{\star}
is achieved by applying time sharing.
Hence, given distributions
P_{X},P_{Y}
,
we here show that for every rate-pair
(R_{1},R_{2})\in\mathcal{R}_{P_{X},P_{Y}}
there exists a
(n,2^{nR_{1}},2^{nR_{2}},\varepsilon_{n})
random code
\mathcal{C}_{n}
for which the average error probability, averaged over the choice of code
\mathcal{C}_{n}
satisfies the following:

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]=0.

(122)

We now start with the randomized construction for
\mathcal{C}_{n}
.

VII-B
1
Randomized Encoder Construction

We choose the rate pair
(R_{1},R_{2})
satisfying

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle<\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q},

\displaystyle R_{2}

\displaystyle<\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q},

\displaystyle R_{1}+R_{2}

\displaystyle<\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}.

Then, we set the pair
(\widehat{R}_{1},\widehat{R}_{2})
as

\displaystyle R_{1}<\widehat{R}_{1}

\displaystyle<\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q},

(123)

\displaystyle R_{2}<\widehat{R}_{2}

\displaystyle<\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q},

(124)

\displaystyle R_{1}+R_{2}<\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2}

\displaystyle<\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}.

(125)

Then, we choose sufficiently small real numbers

t,\varepsilon\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\widehat{R}_{1}

\displaystyle<\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}-\frac{1}{t}\log(1+\varepsilon),

(126)

\displaystyle\widehat{R}_{2}

\displaystyle<\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}-\frac{1}{t}\log(1+\varepsilon),

(127)

\displaystyle\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2}

\displaystyle<\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}-\frac{1}{t}\log(1+\varepsilon),

(128)

where
\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}
is chosen in (
2
).

Given a distribution
P_{X}
on
\mathcal{X}
,
Alice randomly generates
2^{nR_{1}}
sequences
\{X^{n}(m_{1})\in\mathcal{X}^{n}:m_{1}\in[2^{nR_{1}}]\}
according to a probability distribution
P_{X}^{n}
.
Similarly, given a distribution
P_{Y}
on
\mathcal{Y}
,
Bob randomly generates
2^{nR_{2}}
sequences
\{Y^{n}(m_{2})\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}:m_{2}\in[2^{nR_{2}}]\}
according to a probability distribution
P_{Y}^{n}
.
That is, for any element
m_{1}\in[2^{nR_{1}}]
,
the random variables
X^{n}(m_{1})
and
T^{n}(m_{2})
obeys the distributions

\Pr\left\{X^{n}(m_{1})\right\}:=\prod_{i=1}^{n}P_{X}(X_{i}(m_{1}))
and
\Pr\left\{Y^{n}(m_{2})\right\}:=\prod_{i=1}^{n}P_{Y}(Y_{i}(m_{2}))
, where
X^{n}(m_{1}):=\left(X_{1}(m_{1}),\cdots,X_{n}(m_{1})\right)
and
Y^{n}(m_{2}):=\bigl(Y_{1}(m_{2}),\cdots,Y_{n}(m_{2})\bigr)
.

That is, for any element
\forall m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}
,
the random variable
X^{n}(m)
obeys the distribution

\Pr\bigl\{X^{n}(m)\bigr\}:=\prod_{i=1}^{n}P(X_{i}(m))
, where
X^{n}(m):=\left(X_{1}(m),\cdots,X_{n}(m)\right)
.
Then, we randomly choose the encoders
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{2}
subject to the following distribution;

\displaystyle\Pr(\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{1}(m_{1})=X^{n}(m_{1}),\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{2}(m_{2})=Y^{n}(m_{2}),\hbox{ for }m_{1}=1,\ldots,2^{nR_{1}},m_{2}=1,\ldots,2^{nR_{2}})

\displaystyle:=\prod_{m_{1}=1}^{\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}}\Pr\left\{X^{n}(m_{1})\right\}\cdot\prod_{m_{2}=1}^{\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}}\Pr\left\{Y^{n}(m_{2})\right\}.

To send a message
m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}
, Alice encodes it to input sequence
X^{n}(m_{1})
and transmits it over the channel. Similarly, to end a message
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}
, Bob encodes it to input sequence
Y^{n}(m_{2})
and transmits it over the channel.

VII-B
2
Decoding Strategy (Universal Decoder)

Upon receiving the state
\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n},Y^{n},s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}
, Charlie tries to guess the message pair sent simultaneously by measuring the state. We now define the measurement which will be used by Charlie. From (
58
), we consider
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}
and
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}
to be in the following form:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{X}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,x^{n},y^{n}}^{X},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,x^{n},y^{n}}^{Y}.

For each message pair
(m_{1},m_{2})\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\times\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}
, we construct two separate decoders (each for one of messages) as follows,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X}

\displaystyle:=\left(\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}}\Pi^{X}_{\hat{m}_{1}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Pi^{X}_{m_{1}}\left(\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}}\Pi^{X}_{\hat{m}_{1}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}

\displaystyle:=\left(\sum_{\hat{m}_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}}\Pi^{Y}_{\hat{m}_{2}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Pi^{Y}_{m_{2}}\left(\sum_{\hat{m}_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}}\Pi^{Y}_{\hat{m}_{2}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

where
\Pi^{X}_{\hat{m}_{1}}
and
\Pi^{Y}_{\hat{m}_{2}}
are defined as follows

\Pi^{X}_{\hat{m}_{1}}:=\sum_{\hat{m}_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}}\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X^{n}(\hat{m}_{1}),Y^{n}(\hat{m}_{2})}^{X},\quad\Pi^{Y}_{\hat{m}_{2}}:=\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}}\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X^{n}(\hat{m}_{1}),Y^{n}(\hat{m}_{2})}^{Y}.

Charlie then uses the POVM
\left\{\Lambda_{m_{1},m_{2}}^{XY}\right\}_{m_{1},m_{2}}
to decode the message pair
jointly
, where
\Lambda_{m_{1},m_{2}}^{XY}
is defined as follows

\Lambda_{m_{1},m_{2}}^{XY}:=\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X}\right)\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(129)

where

\sum_{m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}}\sum_{m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}}\Lambda_{m_{1},m_{2}}^{XY}=\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}.

Observe that the above POVM is universal as
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,x^{n},y^{n}}^{X}
and
\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,x^{n},y^{n}}^{Y}
are independent of the channel. In the following, we denote the triplet
(\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{1},\mathcal{E}^{(n)}_{2},\left\{\Lambda_{m_{1},m_{2}}^{XY}\right\}_{m_{1},m_{2}})
by
\mathcal{C}_{n}
.

VII-B
3
Error Analysis

We now do the error analysis as follows. For every
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
, the following holds.

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})=\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})

(130)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}-\Lambda_{m_{1},m_{2}}^{XY}\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}-\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X}\right)\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}-\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)-\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X}\right)\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{a}{=}\bar{e}^{Y}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})+\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}-\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X}\right)\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{b}{\leq}\bar{e}^{Y}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})+\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}-\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X}\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}{\left\lVert\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right\rVert}_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{c}{\leq}\bar{e}^{Y}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})+\bar{e}^{X}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})+\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}2\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}-\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{e}^{Y}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})+\bar{e}^{X}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})+\sqrt{\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}2\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}-\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]}

\displaystyle=\bar{e}^{Y}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})+\bar{e}^{X}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})+2\sqrt{\bar{e}^{Y}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})},

(131)

where in equality
a
, we define

\bar{e}^{Y}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n}):=\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}-\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right],

b
follows from Fact
14
and in
c
we define

\bar{e}^{X}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n}):=\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}-\left(\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X}\right)\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right],
and the inequality
c
follows from Fact
9
.
Taking the average with respect to
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}^{Y}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}^{X}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]+2\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\sqrt{\bar{e}^{Y}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}^{Y}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}^{X}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]+2\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}^{Y}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]},

(132)

where we employ Jensen inequality.

We now upper-bound the quantity
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}^{Y}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]
as follows

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}^{Y}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}-\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{Y}\right)\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\overset{a}{\leq}\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}\left(2\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}-\Pi^{Y}_{m_{2}}\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]\right]+4\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{m}_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}:\\
\hat{m}_{2}\neq m_{2}\end{subarray}}\Pi^{Y}_{\hat{m}_{2}}\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}\left(2\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}-\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}}\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X^{n}(\hat{m}_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2})}^{Y}\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]\right]\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 70.0pt\left.+4\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{m}_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}:\\
\hat{m}_{2}\neq m_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}}\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X^{n}(\hat{m}_{1}),Y^{n}(\hat{m}_{2})}^{Y}\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]\right]\right)

\displaystyle\overset{b}{\leq}\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}\left(2\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2})}^{Y}\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]\right]\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 70.0pt\left.+4\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{m}_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}:\\
\hat{m}_{2}\neq m_{2}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(\hat{m}_{2})}^{Y}\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]\right]\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 70.0pt\left.+4\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\times\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\\
:\hat{m}_{1}\neq m_{1},\hat{m}_{2}\neq m_{2}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X^{n}(\hat{m}_{1}),Y^{n}(\hat{m}_{2})}^{Y}\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\\
m_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\end{subarray}}\left(2\mathbb{E}_{X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2})}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2})}^{Y}\right)\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]\right]\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 70.0pt\left.+4\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{m}_{2}\in\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}:\\
\hat{m}_{2}\neq m_{2}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(\hat{m}_{2})}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(\hat{m}_{2})}^{Y}\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}(m_{1}),s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]\right]\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 70.0pt\left.+4\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}\times\mathcal{M}^{2}_{n}\\
:\hat{m}_{1}\neq m_{1},\hat{m}_{2}\neq m_{2}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{X^{n}(\hat{m}_{1}),Y^{n}(\hat{m}_{2})}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X^{n}(\hat{m}_{1}),Y^{n}(\hat{m}_{2})}^{Y}\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]\right]\right)

\displaystyle{\leq}2\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}\right)\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},s^{n}}\right]+4\cdot 2^{nR_{2}}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},s^{n}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+4\cdot 2^{n(R_{1}+R_{2})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}^{Y}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}\\
y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n}\end{subarray}}P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{s^{n}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{c}{\leq}2f(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|,t)(1+\varepsilon)^{n}\left(2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{2}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[Y;\mathcal{B}|X]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)}+2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+4\cdot g_{1}(n,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{n(R_{2}-\widehat{R}_{2})}+4\cdot g_{3}(n,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2}-R_{1}-R_{2})},

(133)

where
a
follows from Fact
12
,
b
follows from the fact that
\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{1}_{n}}\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X^{n}(\hat{m}_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2})}^{Y}\geq\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X^{n}(m_{1}),Y^{n}(m_{2})}^{Y}
and
c
follows from
eqs.

81
,
84
and
85
.

Similarly, using
eqs.

80
,
82
and
83
, we get the following upper-bound for the quantity
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}^{X}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}^{X}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2f(n,\left|\mathcal{X}\right|,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\right|,t)(1+\varepsilon)^{n}\left(2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{1}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[X;\mathcal{B}|Y]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)}+2^{nt\left(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon})}I_{1-t}[XY;\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+4\cdot g_{2}(n,\left|\mathcal{Y}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{n(R_{1}-\widehat{R}_{1})}+4\cdot g_{3}(n,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n(\widehat{R}_{1}+\widehat{R}_{2}-R_{1}-R_{2})}.

(134)

To reduce redundancy in the main script, the proof of (
134
) is given in Appendix
G
.
Due to the conditions (
123
) – (
128
),
the pair of (
133
) and (
134
) implies

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}^{Y}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]=0,\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}^{X}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]=0.

(135)

Then, the combination of
(
132
) and (
135
)
guarantees (
122
).
This completes the proof for achievability using the non-simultaneous testing approach, mentioned in Lemma
5
.

VII-C

Converse part of Theorem
3

Let
\mathcal{C}_{n}
be a
(n,2^{nR_{1}},2^{nR_{2}},\varepsilon_{n})
random code satisfying

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]=0.

(136)

For any any probability distribution
Q
over
\mathcal{S}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right]\geq\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}\left[\sum_{s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}Q^{n}(s^{n})\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\right].

(137)

Hence, the RHS of the above also goes to zero.
We consider a classical-quantum multiple access channel (CQ-MAC)
\mathcal{W}_{mac}^{Q^{n}}:=\sum_{s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}Q^{n}(s^{n})\mathcal{N}^{X^{n}Y^{n}\to B^{n}}_{s^{n}}
.
Then, the joint state induced on
X^{n}
,
Y^{n}
and
\mathcal{B}^{n}
is represented by the following CQ state,

\rho^{X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X},P_{Y},Q^{n}}:=\sum_{x^{n}\in\mathcal{X}^{n},y^{n}\in\mathcal{Y}^{n},s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}Q^{n}(s^{n})P^{n}_{X}(x^{n})\overset{X^{n}}{|x^{n}\rangle\langle x^{n}|}\otimes P^{n}_{Y}(y^{n})\overset{Y^{n}}{|y^{n}\rangle\langle y^{n}|}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n},y^{n},s^{n}}.

(138)

All the information-theoretic quantities below are calculated with respect to (
138
).
We will prove a converse under the MAC
\mathcal{W}_{mac}^{Q^{n}}
.
This will imply an outer bound on the random code capacity region for the CQ-AVMAC (mentioned in Definition
20
).

Let
(M_{1},M_{2})
be two independent and uniformly distributed random variables over
[2^{nR_{1}}]\times[2^{nR_{2}}]
and let
(\hat{M}_{1},\hat{M_{2}})
be an estimation of
(M_{1},M_{2})
. The diagram below shows the general process of communicating the message pair
(M_{1}M_{2})
over
\mathcal{W}_{mac}^{Q^{n}}
.

(M_{1},M_{2})\longrightarrow(X^{n},Y^{n})\underset{\mathcal{W}_{mac}^{Q^{n}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{B}^{n}\longrightarrow(\hat{M}_{1},\hat{M_{2}}).

Fano’s inequality (Fact
15
) implies

\displaystyle H(M_{1}M_{2}|\hat{M}_{1}\hat{M_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{n})

\displaystyle\leq n(R_{1}+R_{2})\Pr\left\{(M_{1},M_{2})\neq(\hat{M}_{1},\hat{M_{2}})\right\}+H_{b}\left(\Pr\left\{(M_{1},M_{2})\neq(\hat{M}_{1},\hat{M_{2}})\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n(R_{1}+R_{2})\varepsilon_{n}+H_{b}(\varepsilon_{n})

\displaystyle\overset{a}{=}n\delta_{n},

(139)

where in
a
, we define
\delta_{n}
as follows

\delta_{n}:=(R_{1}+R_{2})\varepsilon_{n}+\frac{1}{n}H_{b}(\varepsilon_{n}).

(140)

Observe that,
\lim_{n\to\infty}\delta_{n}=0
. We now upper-bound the rate
R_{1}
as follows

\displaystyle nR_{1}

\displaystyle=H(M_{1}|\mathcal{C}_{n})

\displaystyle=I[M_{1};\hat{M}_{1}M_{2}\hat{M}_{2}\mathcal{C}_{n}]+H(M_{1}|\hat{M}_{1}M_{2}\hat{M}_{2}\mathcal{C}_{n})

\displaystyle\leq I[M_{1};\hat{M}_{1}M_{2}\hat{M}_{2}|\mathcal{C}_{n}]+H(M_{1},M_{2}|\hat{M}_{1}\hat{M}_{2}\mathcal{C}_{n})

\displaystyle\overset{b}{\leq}I[X^{n};Y^{n}\mathcal{B}^{n}|\mathcal{C}_{n}]+n\delta_{n}

\displaystyle\overset{c}{=}I[X^{n};\mathcal{B}^{n}|Y^{n}\mathcal{C}_{n}]+n\delta_{n}

\displaystyle=H(\mathcal{B}^{n}|Y^{n}\mathcal{C}_{n})-H(\mathcal{B}^{n}|X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{C}_{n})+n\delta_{n}

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}H(\mathcal{B}^{n}|Y^{n}\mathcal{C}_{n})-\sum_{i=1}^{n}H(B|X_{i},Y_{i}\mathcal{C}_{n})+n\delta_{n}

\displaystyle\overset{e}{\leq}\sum_{i=1}^{n}H(B|Y_{i}\mathcal{C}_{n})-\sum_{i=1}^{n}H(B|X_{i},Y_{i}\mathcal{C}_{n})+n\delta_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}I[X_{i}:\mathcal{B}|Y_{i}\mathcal{C}_{n}]_{P_{X_{i}},P_{Y_{i}},Q}+n\delta_{n},

where
b
follows from data processing inequality
[
60
, Theorem 4.3.3]
and (
139
),
c
follows from the fact that
X^{n}
and
Y^{n}
are independent,
d
follows from the fact that
\rho_{x^{n},y^{n},Q^{n}}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}
can be written as
\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{x_{i},y_{i},Q}^{\mathcal{B}}
(where for any

x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y},\rho_{x_{i},y_{i},Q}^{\mathcal{B}}:=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}Q(s)\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}
) and
e
follows from chain rule for entropy and the fact that removing the conditioning increases the entropy. Since the chosen
Q
is any distribution over
\mathcal{S}
therefore, we get the following upper-bound on
R_{1}
by optimizing over the choice of
Q
,

\displaystyle R_{1}-\delta_{n}\leq
