\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[X_{i}:\mathcal{B}|Y_{i}\mathcal{C}_{n}]_{P_{X_{i}},P_{Y_{i}},Q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{c}P_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}(c)\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[X_{i}:\mathcal{B}|Y_{i}\mathcal{C}_{n}]_{P_{X_{i}|\mathcal{C}_{n}=c},P_{Y_{i}|\mathcal{C}_{n}=c},Q}.

(141)

Similarly, the rate
R_{2}
is upper-bounded as follows

\displaystyle R_{2}-\delta_{n}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[Y_{i}:\mathcal{B}|X_{i}\mathcal{C}_{n}]_{P_{X_{i}},P_{Y_{i}},Q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{c}P_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}(c)\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[Y_{i}:\mathcal{B}|X_{i}\mathcal{C}_{n}]_{P_{X_{i}|\mathcal{C}_{n}=c},P_{Y_{i}|\mathcal{C}_{n}=c},Q}.

(142)

The sum rate
(R_{1}+R_{2})
is upper-bounded as follows

\displaystyle n(R_{1}+R_{2})

\displaystyle=H(M_{1}M_{2}|\mathcal{C}_{n})

\displaystyle=I[M_{1}M_{2};\hat{M}_{1}\hat{M}_{2}|\mathcal{C}_{n}]+H(M_{1}M_{2}|\hat{M}_{1}\hat{M}_{2}\mathcal{C}_{n})

\displaystyle\overset{f}{\leq}I[X^{n}Y^{n}:\mathcal{B}^{n}|\mathcal{C}_{n}]+n\delta_{n}

\displaystyle=H(\mathcal{B}^{n})-H(\mathcal{B}^{n}|X^{n}Y^{n}\mathcal{C}_{n})+n\delta_{n}

\displaystyle\overset{g}{=}H(\mathcal{B}^{n}|\mathcal{C}_{n})-\sum_{i=1}^{n}H(\mathcal{B}|X_{i}Y_{i}\mathcal{C}_{n})+n\delta_{n}

\displaystyle\leq nH(\mathcal{B}|\mathcal{C}_{n})-\sum_{i=1}^{n}H(\mathcal{B}|X_{i}Y_{i}\mathcal{C}_{n})+n\delta_{n}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}I[X_{i}Y_{i}:\mathcal{B}|\mathcal{C}_{n}]_{P_{X_{i}},P_{Y_{i}},Q}+n\delta_{n},

where
f
follows from data processing inequality
[
60
, Theorem 4.3.3]
and (
139
),
g
from the fact that
\rho_{x^{n},y^{n},Q^{n}}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}
can be written as
\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\rho_{x_{i},y_{i},Q}^{\mathcal{B}}
(where for any
x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y},\rho_{x_{i},y_{i},Q}^{\mathcal{B}}:=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}Q(s)\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}
). Since the chosen
Q
is any distribution over
\mathcal{S}
therefore, we get the following upper-bound on
(R_{1}+R_{2})
by optimizing over the choice of
Q
,

\displaystyle(R_{1}-\delta_{n})+(R_{2}-\delta_{n})\leq R_{1}+R_{2}-\delta_{n}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[X_{i}Y_{i}:\mathcal{B}|\mathcal{C}_{n}]_{P_{X_{i}},P_{Y_{i}},Q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{c}P_{\mathcal{C}_{n}}(c)\inf_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[X_{i}Y_{i}:\mathcal{B}]_{P_{X_{i}|\mathcal{C}_{n}=c},P_{Y_{i}|\mathcal{C}_{n}=c},Q}.

(143)

The combination of (
141
), (
142
), and (
143
)
guarantees that
(R_{1}-\delta_{n},R_{2}-\delta_{n})
belongs to
\mathcal{R}^{\star}
.
Since
\delta_{n}\to 0
,

(R_{1},R_{2})
also belongs to
\mathcal{R}^{\star}
.
This completes the proof of Theorem
3
.

In the subsection below we discuss the generalization of our achievability techniques to the case of
T(T>2)
senders.

VII-D

Discussion on the achievability techniques for the case of
T

(T>2)
senders

VII-D
1
Formulation

In this subsection, for simplicity, we assume that

|\mathcal{S}|<\infty
.
To address the case of
T

(T>2)
senders, we formulate CQ-AVMAC with this case as follows.
For the case of CQ-AVMAC with
T
senders we will denote each of the classical input systems as
X_{[T]}:=\{X_{i}\}_{i\in[T]}
and the output quantum system as
\mathcal{B}
. The channel output is represented by a collection
\{\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{x_{[T]},s}\}_{x_{[T]}\in\mathcal{X}_{[T]},s\in\mathcal{S}}
, where
\mathcal{X}_{[T]}:=(\times_{i\in[T]}\mathcal{X}_{i})
(where
\left|\mathcal{X}_{i}\right|<\infty
),
x_{[T]}:=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{T})
, for each state
s\in\mathcal{S}
,
\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{x_{[T]},s}
is the output of the channel corresponding to state
s
, upon inputting
x_{[T]}
. We can represent the joint quantum state induced on
X_{[T]}
and
\mathcal{B}
using the following CQ-states:

\displaystyle\rho^{X^{n}_{[T]}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X_{[T]}},s^{n}}:=\sum_{x^{n}_{[T]}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}_{[T]}}\prod_{i=1}^{[T]}P^{n}_{X_{i}}(x^{n}_{i})\bigotimes_{i=1}^{T}\overset{X^{n}_{i}}{|x^{n}_{i}\rangle\langle x^{n}_{i}|}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}_{[T]},s^{n}},

where
X^{n}_{[T]}:=\{X^{n}_{i}\}_{i\in[T]}
,
P_{X_{[T]}}:=\{P_{X_{1}},\cdots,P_{X_{T}}\}
,
\mathcal{X}^{n}_{[T]}:=(\times_{i=1}^{T}\mathcal{X}^{n}_{i})
,
x^{n}_{[T]}:=\{x^{n}_{i}\}_{i\in[T]}
, where for each
i\in[T]
,
x^{n}_{i}:=(x_{i,1},\cdots,x_{i,n})
,
P_{X_{i}}
is a probability distribution over
\mathcal{X}_{i}
and
P^{n}_{X_{i}}(x^{n}_{i}):=\prod_{j=1}^{n}P_{X_{i}}(x_{i,j})
. For any
x^{n}_{[T]}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}_{[T]}
and
s^{n}:=(s_{1},\cdots,s_{n})\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
, we define
\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}_{[T]},s^{n}}:=\otimes_{j=1}^{n}\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{\{x_{i,j}\}_{i\in[T]},s_{j}}
and
\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{\{x_{i,j}\}_{i\in[T]},s_{j}}
is the output of the channel corresponding to
s_{j}
upon inputting
\{x_{i,j}\}_{i\in[T]}
into it.

We assume that for each
i\in[T]
,
i
-th sender has a message set
\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}:=[2^{nR_{i}}]
. For a particular message tuple
m_{[T]}:=(m_{1},\cdots,m_{T})
chosen by the
T
senders respectively and
given an encoder
\mathcal{E}^{(n),T}
and an element
s^{n}

and a decoding POVM
\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}}
,
we define the probability of error as

\displaystyle e(m_{[T]},\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\right)\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\right].

(144)

Then, we define its average error probability as

\bar{e}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}}):=\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{i\in[T]}R_{i})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{i\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n})}e(m_{[T]},\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}}).

VII-D
2
Generalization of Techniques Based on Lemma
5

To construct our decoder,
we next discuss the generalization of the techniques based on Lemma
5
.
For any
\widehat{R}_{1},\cdots,\widehat{R}_{T}>0
and each
V\subset[T]
, we define the following tests (projectors):

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X_{V}}:=\sum_{x^{n}_{[T]}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}_{[T]}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{T}\overset{X^{n}_{i}}{|x^{n}_{i}\rangle\langle x^{n}_{i}|}\otimes\left\{\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}_{[T]}}\succeq 2^{n\sum_{t\in[T]\setminus V}\widehat{R}_{t}}\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}_{V}}\right\},

where for any
V\subset[T]:V\neq\varnothing
,
\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}_{V}}
is defined using the same techniques used in
eqs.

53
and
54
and for
V=\varnothing
,
\widehat{\rho}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x^{n}_{V}}:=\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{U,n}
(which is defined in (
21
)) and
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X_{V}}\triangleq\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{n}
. For each
i\in[T]
, as a generalization of (
58
), we define a projector
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}^{X_{i}}_{n}
as follows,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{T}}^{X_{i}}_{n}:=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\\
V\subseteq[T]\setminus\{i\}\end{subarray}}{\bullet}\widehat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X_{V}},

(145)

where
\bullet
denotes iterative matrix multiplication (see Definition
10
). Using similar techniques used in the proof of Lemma
5
, one can show that
for each
i\in[T]
and each
t\in(0,1)
,
the projector
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}^{X_{i}}_{n}
satisfies the following:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{X^{n}_{[T]}\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\widehat{\mathbb{T}}^{X_{i}}_{n}\right)\rho^{X^{n}_{[T]}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X_{[T]}},s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\hat{f}(n,\left|\mathcal{X}_{1}\right|,\cdots,\left|\mathcal{X}_{T}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}\right|)\sum_{V\subseteq[T]:i\in V}2^{n\left(\sum_{v\in V}\widehat{R}_{v}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I_{1-t}[X_{V};B|X_{[T]\setminus V}]_{P_{X_{[T]}},Q}\right)},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\widehat{\mathbb{T}}^{X_{i}}_{n}\left(\rho^{X^{n}_{M}}_{P_{X_{M}}}\otimes\rho^{X^{n}_{[T]\setminus M}\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{P_{X_{[T]\setminus M}},s^{n}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\hat{g}(n,\prod_{t\in[T]\setminus M}\left|\mathcal{X}_{t}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n\left(\sum_{u\in M}\widehat{R}_{u}\right)},\quad\forall M\subseteq[T]:i\in M,

where for each
V\subseteq[T]
and probability distributions
Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})
, we define
I_{1-t}[X_{V};B|X_{[T]\setminus V}]_{P_{X_{[T]}},Q}
as follows,

\displaystyle I_{1-t}[X_{V};B|X_{[T]\setminus V}]_{P_{X_{[T]}},Q}

\displaystyle:=\min_{\{\mu_{x_{[T]\setminus V}}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\otimes n})\}_{x_{[T]\setminus V}}}D_{1-t}\left(\rho^{X_{[T]}\mathcal{B}}_{P_{X_{[T]}},Q}\left\|\rho^{X_{V}}_{P_{X_{V}}}\otimes\left(\sum_{x_{[T]\setminus V}\in\mathcal{X}_{[T]\setminus V}}\prod_{j\in[T]\setminus V}P_{X_{j}}(x_{j})\bigotimes_{j\in[T]\setminus V}\overset{X_{j}}{|x_{j}\rangle\langle x_{j}|}\otimes\mu_{x_{[T]\setminus V}}\right.\right)\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1-t}{t}\log\left(\sum_{x_{[T]\setminus V}\in\mathcal{X}_{[T]\setminus V}}\prod_{j\in[T]\setminus V}P_{X_{j}}(x_{j})\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\sum_{x_{V}\in\mathcal{X}_{V}}\prod_{k\in V}P_{X_{k}}(x_{k})(\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{Q,x_{[T]}})^{1-t}\right)^{\frac{1}{1-t}}\right]\right),

\displaystyle\rho^{X_{[T]}\mathcal{B}}_{P_{X_{[T]}},Q}:=\sum_{x_{[T]}\in\mathcal{X}_{[T]}}\prod_{i=1}^{[T]}P_{X_{i}}(x_{i})\bigotimes_{i=1}^{T}\overset{X_{i}}{|x_{i}\rangle\langle x_{i}|}\otimes\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{x_{[T]},Q},

where the symbols used here are defined as

\rho^{\mathcal{B}}_{x_{[T]},Q}:=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}Q(s)\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{x_{[T]},s}
, and

\displaystyle\hat{f}(n,\left|\mathcal{X}_{1}\right|,\cdots,\left|\mathcal{X}_{T}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}\right|)

\displaystyle:=(n+1)^{\left|\mathcal{S}\right|-1+\frac{t(\prod_{i\in[T]}\left|\mathcal{X}_{i}\right|)(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}{2}+(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)t(\prod_{i\in[T]}\left|\mathcal{X}_{i}\right|)},

\displaystyle\hat{g}(n,\prod_{t\in[T]\setminus M}\left|\mathcal{X}_{t}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)

\displaystyle:=n^{\frac{(\prod_{t\in[T]\setminus M}\left|\mathcal{X}_{t}\right|)\left|\mathcal{B}\right|(\left|\mathcal{B}\right|-1)}{2}}\left|\Lambda^{n}_{\left|\mathcal{B}\right|}\right|^{(\prod_{t\in[T]\setminus M}\left|\mathcal{X}_{t}\right|)}.

VII-D
3
Decoding Strategy

Upon receiving the state
\rho_{\begin{subarray}{c}X^{n}_{[T]},s^{n}\end{subarray}}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}
(where
s^{n}
is unknown to the receiver), the receiver then tries to guess the message pair sent using certain measurement based decoder. Now from (
145
), for each
i\in[T]
, we assume that
\widehat{\mathbb{T}}^{X_{i}}_{n}
has the following form:

\widehat{\mathbb{T}}^{X_{i}}_{n}:=\sum_{x^{n}_{[T]}\in\mathcal{X}^{n}_{[T]}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{T}\overset{X^{n}_{i}}{|x^{n}_{i}\rangle\langle x^{n}_{i}|}\otimes\widehat{\mathbb{T}}^{X_{i}}_{n,x^{n}_{[T]}}.

For each message
T
-tuple
m_{[T]}\in(\times_{i\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n})
, we construct
T
separate decoders (each for one of messages). For each
i\in[T]
, we define a separate decoder for
i
-th sender as follows,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{m_{i}}^{X_{i}}

\displaystyle:=\left(\sum_{\hat{m}_{i}\in\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}}\Pi^{X_{i}}_{\hat{m}_{i}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Pi^{X_{i}}_{m_{i}}\left(\sum_{\hat{m}_{i}\in\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}}\Pi^{X_{i}}_{\hat{m}_{i}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(146)

where for any
\hat{m}_{i}\in\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}
,
\Pi^{X_{i}}_{\hat{m}_{i}}
is defined as follows,

\Pi^{X_{i}}_{\hat{m}_{i}}:=\sum_{\hat{m}_{[T]\setminus i}\in(\times_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\in[T]\setminus i\end{subarray}}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}\hat{\mathbb{T}}_{n,X^{n}_{[T]}(\hat{m}_{[T]})},

(147)

where
\hat{m}_{[T]}:(\hat{m}_{1},\cdots,\hat{m}_{T})

and
\hat{m}_{[T]\setminus i}:=(\hat{m}_{j})_{j\in[T]:j\neq i}
. The receiver then uses the POVM
\left\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\right\}_{m_{[T]}}
to decode the message jointly, where
\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}
is defined as follows,

\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}:=\left(\Lambda_{m_{T}}^{X_{T}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Lambda_{m_{T-1}}^{X_{T-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdots\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{X_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X_{1}}\right)\left(\Lambda_{m_{2}}^{X_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdots\left(\Lambda_{m_{T-1}}^{X_{T-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\Lambda_{m_{T}}^{Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(148)

It is important to note that, for each
m_{[T]}\in(\times_{i\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n})
,
the matrices
\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X_{1}},\cdots,\Lambda_{m_{T}}^{X_{T}}
commute with each other. Thus, the following holds,

\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{i\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n})}\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}=\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}}.

Now, if the receiver gets the measurement outcome corresponding to
\Lambda_{\hat{m}_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}
, the receiver then declares the message tuple to be
\hat{m}_{[T]}
.
Under the above choice of the decoder,
we simplify

e(m_{[T]},\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})
and

\bar{e}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})
to

e(m_{[T]},\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n})
and

\bar{e}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n})
.

In the next part, we will show the characterization of rate-tuples
(R_{1},\cdots,R_{T})
, for which there exists
an
(n,2^{nR_{1}},\cdots,2^{nR_{T}},\varepsilon_{n})
random code with the encoder
\mathcal{E}^{(n),T}
for which the average error probability, averaged over the choice of encoder
\mathcal{E}^{(n),T}
satisfies

\max_{s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{E}^{(n),T}}\left[\bar{e}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n})\right]<\varepsilon_{n}
, where
\lim_{n\to\infty}\varepsilon_{n}=0
. We now start with the construction of the encoder.

VII-D
4
Randomized Encoder Construction

For each
i\in[T]
and a probability distribution
P_{X_{i}}
on
\mathcal{X}_{i}
,
the
i
-th sender randomly generates
2^{nR_{i}}
sequences
\{X^{n}_{i}(m_{i})\in\mathcal{X}^{n}_{i}:m_{i}\in[2^{nR_{i}}]\}
according to
the probability distribution
P_{X_{i}}^{n}
.
That is, for any element

m_{i}\in[2^{nR_{i}}]
, the random variable
X^{n}_{i}(m_{i})
obeys the distribution

\Pr\left\{X^{n}_{i}(m_{i})\right\}:=\prod_{j=1}^{n}P_{X_{i}}(X_{i,j}(m_{i}))
, where
X^{n}_{i}(m_{i}):=\left(X_{i,1}(m_{i}),\cdots,X_{i,n}(m_{i})\right)
.
Then, we randomly choose the encoder
\mathcal{E}^{(n),T}
subject to the following distribution;

\Pr(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T}_{i}(m_{i})=X_{i}^{n}(m)\hbox{ for }i=1,\ldots,T,~m_{i}=1,\ldots,2^{nR_{i}}):=\prod_{i=1}^{T}\prod_{m_{i}=1}^{\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n}}\Pr\left\{X^{n}_{i}(m_{i})\right\}.

If the
T
senders have to send a message tuple
m_{[T]}:=(m_{1},m_{2},\cdots,m_{T})\in(\times_{i\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n})
, for each
i\in[T]
,
i
-th sender encodes
m_{i}
to the input sequence
X^{n}_{i}(m_{i})
and
sends it over the channel.

VII-D
5
Error Analysis

Using the fact that for each
m_{[T]}\in(\times_{i\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{i}_{n})
,
\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X_{1}},\cdots,\Lambda_{m_{T}}^{X_{T}}
commutes with each other, we can show that the average error probability
for any particular
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
is of the following form:

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt\bar{e}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{t\in[T]}R_{t})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{j\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}e(m_{[T]},\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{t\in[T]}R_{t})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{j\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\right)\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{t\in[T]}R_{t})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{j\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\Lambda_{m_{T}}^{X_{T}}\right)\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{t\in[T]}R_{t})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{j\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\left(\Lambda_{m_{T}}^{X_{T}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\left(\Lambda_{m_{T-1}}^{X_{T-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdots\left(\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X_{1}}\right)\cdots\left(\Lambda_{m_{T-1}}^{X_{T-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\left(\Lambda_{m_{T}}^{Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{a}{\leq}\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{t\in[T]}R_{t})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{j\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\Lambda_{m_{T}}^{X_{T}}\right)\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{t\in[T]}R_{t})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{j\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\left(\Lambda_{m_{T-1}}^{X_{T-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdots\left(\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X_{1}}\right)\cdots\left(\Lambda_{m_{T-1}}^{X_{T-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{t\in[T]}R_{t})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{j\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}{\left\lVert\left(\Lambda_{m_{T}}^{Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\left(\Lambda_{m_{T}}^{Y}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\right\rVert}_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{b}{\leq}\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{t\in[T]}R_{t})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{j\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\Lambda_{m_{T}}^{X_{T}}\right)\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{t\in[T]}R_{t})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{j\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\left(\Lambda_{m_{T-1}}^{X_{T-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdots\left(\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X_{1}}\right)\cdots\left(\Lambda_{m_{T-1}}^{X_{T-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+2\sqrt{\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{t\in[T]}R_{t})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{j\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\Lambda_{m_{T}}^{X_{T}}\right)\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\right]}

\displaystyle\overset{c}{=}\bar{e}^{X_{T}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})+2\sqrt{\bar{e}^{X_{T}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{t\in[T]}R_{t})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{j\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\left(\Lambda_{m_{T-1}}^{X_{T-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdots\left(\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X_{1}}\right)\cdots\left(\Lambda_{m_{T-1}}^{X_{T-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{d}{\leq}\bar{e}^{X_{T}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})+\bar{e}^{X_{T-1}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})+2\sqrt{\bar{e}^{X_{T}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+2\sqrt{\bar{e}^{X_{T-1}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{t\in[T]}R_{t})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{j\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\left(\Lambda_{m_{T-2}}^{X_{T-2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdots\left(\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X_{1}}\right)\cdots\left(\Lambda_{m_{T-2}}^{X_{T-2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[T]:\\
i\neq 1\end{subarray}}\bar{e}^{X_{i}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})+2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[T]:\\
i\neq 1\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\bar{e}^{X_{i}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt+\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{t\in[T]}R_{t})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{j\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\Lambda_{m_{1}}^{X_{1}}\right)\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[T]\end{subarray}}\bar{e}^{X_{i}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})+2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq 1\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\bar{e}^{X_{i}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})},

where
a
follows from Fact
14
,
b
follows from Fact
9
, in equality
c
, for any
i\in[T]
, we define

\bar{e}^{X_{i}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}}):=\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{t\in[T]}R_{t})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{j\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\Lambda_{m_{i}}^{X_{i}}\right)\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\right],

d
follows from repertitve calculations performed in earlier steps. Taking the expectation with respect to
\mathcal{E}^{(n),T}
, for any particular
s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
we have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{E}^{(n),T}}\left[\bar{e}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})\right]\leq\sum_{i\in[T]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{E}^{(n),T}}\left[\bar{e}^{X_{i}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})\right]+2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\neq 1\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{E}^{(n),T}}\left[\bar{e}^{X_{i}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n},\{\Lambda_{m_{[T]}}^{X_{[T]}}\}_{m_{[T]}})\right]},

(149)

where for each
i\in[T]
, we define the individual error term
\bar{e}^{X_{i}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n})
corresponding to
i
-th sender as follows,

\bar{e}^{X_{i}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n}):=\frac{1}{2^{n(\sum_{j\in[T]}R_{j})}}\sum_{m_{[T]}\in(\times_{j\in[T]}\mathcal{M}^{j}_{n})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{I}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}-\Lambda_{m_{i}}^{X_{i}}\right)\rho^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}_{X^{n}_{[T]}(m_{[T]}),s^{n}}\right].

The derivation of (
149
) has not been included in the manuscript since it can be directly shown by iterating the derivations of (
132
) for
T-1
times. Now using similar arguments that were used to prove (
133
), for each
i\in[T]
, we show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{E}^{(n),T}}\left[\bar{e}^{X_{i}}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n})\right]\leq

\displaystyle 2\hat{f}(n,\left|\mathcal{X}_{1}\right|,\cdots,\left|\mathcal{X}_{T}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|,\left|\mathcal{S}\right|)\sum_{V\subset[T]:i\in V}2^{n\left(\sum_{v\in V}\widehat{R}_{v}-\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I_{1-t}[X_{V};B|X_{[T]\setminus V}]_{P_{X_{[T]}},Q}\right)}

\displaystyle+4\sum_{M\subset[T]:i\in M}\hat{g}(n,\prod_{t\in[T]\setminus M}\left|\mathcal{X}_{t}\right|,\left|\mathcal{B}\right|)2^{-n\left(\sum_{u\in M}\widehat{R}_{u}\right)}.

Thus, for sufficiently large
n
,
\max_{s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{E}^{(n),T}}\left[\bar{e}(\mathcal{E}^{(n),T},s^{n})\right]
goes to zero, if we choose a rate tuple
(R_{1},\cdots,R_{T})
, which satisfies the following:

\displaystyle\sum_{v\in V}R_{v}<\min_{Q\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{S})}I[X_{V};B|X_{[T]\setminus V}]_{P_{X_{[T]}},Q},\quad\quad\forall V\subseteq[T]:V\neq\varnothing.

This gives a proof of achievability for the capacity region of
T
-sender CQ-AVMAC under random codes using an extension of the non-simultaneous testing approach for generalized independence testing discussed in subsection
VI-B
.

The achievability technique based on Lemma
7
(also see Appendix
F
) can be easily generalized using similar techniques required to prove
[
42
, Corollary 4]
.

The code obtained through these techniques can be derandomized by defining
2^{T}-1
symmetrizablity conditions mentioned in
eqs.

150
,
151
and
152
.

VIII
Deterministic Code Capacity Region of CQ-AVMAC

VIII-A

Characterization of Deterministic Code Capacity Region

In the previous sections, we derived
the random code capacity of CQ-AVC in Theorem
2
and
the random code capacity region
\mathcal{R}_{r}
of CQ-AVMAC in Theorem
3
.
However, unlike the random code capacity and the random code capacity region,
the deterministic code capacity and
the deterministic code capacity region of CQ-AVMAC may be empty sometimes.
In this section, we only consider the
deterministic code capacity with two senders, where
Alice (sender 1) and Bob (sender 2) have input variables

X
and
Y
, respectively.
The case with a single sender can be easily recovered
by the following modification;
We set
Y
to be a singleton variable.
We define the topological interior
\mathbf{Int}\{A\}
of
a set
A\subset\mathbb{R}\times\{0\}

as a subset
\mathbb{R}\times\{0\}
.

To discuss this more formally, we first introduce a notion of symmetrizability in Definition
21

for a discrete set
\mathcal{S}

(a similar notion was also discussed in the classical case in
[
19
]
). We then use this definition to establish a necessary and sufficient condition for
\mathbf{Int}\{\mathcal{R}_{d}\}=\varnothing
in Lemma
8
.

Finally we use Lemma
8
to derandomize the code obtained in Theorem
3
and show that either
\mathbf{Int}\{\mathcal{R}_{d}\}=\varnothing
or
\mathcal{R}_{d}=\mathcal{R}_{r}
.

Definition 21
.

(Symmetrizability for a discrete set
\mathcal{S}
)
Assume that
\mathcal{S}
is a discrete set.
A CQ-AVMAC

\left\{\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}:x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y},s\in\mathcal{S}\right\}

is called symmetrizable if at least one of the following conditions holds.

•

(
\mathcal{X}-
symmetrizable) There exists a set of probability distributions
\{U_{1}(.|x)\}_{x\in\mathcal{X}}
over
\mathcal{S}
such that
any two elements
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}
satisfy the following condition;

\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}U_{1}(s|x^{\prime})\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}U_{1}(s|x)\rho_{x^{\prime},y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}.

(150)

•

(
\mathcal{Y}-
symmetrizable) There exists a set of probability distributions
\{U_{2}(.|y)\}_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}
over
\mathcal{S}
such that
any two elements
y,y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}
satisfy the following condition;

\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}U_{2}(s|y^{\prime})\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}U_{2}(s|y)\rho_{x,y^{\prime},s}^{\mathcal{B}}.

(151)

•

(
\mathcal{X}\mathcal{Y}-
symmetrizable) There exists a set of probability distributions
\{U_{3}(.|x,y)\}_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}}
over
\mathcal{S}
such that
any four elements
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}
and
y,y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}
satisfy the following condition;

\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}U_{3}(s|x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}}U_{3}(s|x,y)\rho_{x^{\prime},y^{\prime},s}^{\mathcal{B}}.

(152)

This definition is generalized to a general measurable space
\mathcal{S}

as follows,

Definition 22
.

(Symmetrizability for a general measurable space
\mathcal{S}
)
Assume that
\mathcal{S}
is a general measurable space.
A CQ-AVMAC

\left\{\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}:x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y},s\in\mathcal{S}\right\}

is called symmetrizable if at least one of the following conditions holds.

•

(
\mathcal{X}-
symmetrizable)
The relation

\theta_{1}:=\inf_{U_{1}(.|x)}\max_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y},z\in\mathcal{Z}}\left\|\int_{\mathcal{S}}U_{1}(ds|x^{\prime})\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}-\int_{\mathcal{S}}U_{1}(ds|x)\rho_{x^{\prime},y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}\right\|_{1}=0

(153)

holds, where

\{U_{1}(.|x)\}_{x\in\mathcal{X}}
is
a set of probability measures over
\mathcal{S}
.

•

(
\mathcal{Y}-
symmetrizable)
The relation

\theta_{2}:=\inf_{U_{2}(.|y)}\max_{x\in\mathcal{X},y,y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y},z\in\mathcal{Z}}\left\|\int_{\mathcal{S}}U_{2}(ds|y^{\prime})\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}-\int_{\mathcal{S}}U_{2}(ds|y)\rho_{x,y^{\prime},s}^{\mathcal{B}}\right\|_{1}=0

(154)

holds, where

\{U_{2}(.|y)\}_{y\in\mathcal{Y}}
is
a set of probability measures over
\mathcal{S}
.

•

(
\mathcal{X}\mathcal{Y}-
symmetrizable)
The relation

\theta_{3}:=\inf_{U_{3}(.|x,y)}\max_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X},y,y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y},z\in\mathcal{Z}}\left\|\int_{\mathcal{S}}U_{3}(ds|x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}-\int_{\mathcal{S}}U_{3}(ds|x,y)\rho_{x^{\prime},y^{\prime},s}^{\mathcal{B}}\right\|_{1}=0

(155)

holds, where

\{U_{3}(.|x,y)\}_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}}
is
a set of probability measures over
\mathcal{S}
.

Using the above definition, we obtain the following lemma.

Lemma 8
.

Assume that
\mathcal{S}
is a general measurable space
and satisfies Assumption
1
.
The relation

\mathbf{Int}\{\mathcal{R}_{d}\}=\varnothing
holds if and only if
CQ-AVMAC is symmetrizable.

This lemma is shown in subsections
VIII-B
and
VIII-C
.
We also have the following theorem when
|\mathcal{S}|<\infty
.

Theorem
4
.

(
Deterministic Code Capacity Region
)
Assume that
|\mathcal{S}|<\infty
.
If
\mathbf{Int}\{\mathcal{R}_{d}\}\neq\varnothing
, then
\mathcal{R}_{d}=\mathcal{R}_{r}
.

Therefore, when CQ-AVMAC is not symmetrizable,
the capacity region of deterministic codes equals
the capacity region of randomized codes.
The proof of Theorem
4
directly follows from the following two lemmas mentioned below.

Lemma 9
.

\mathcal{R}_{d}\subseteq\mathcal{R}_{r}.

Proof.

Since any deterministic code
\mathcal{C}_{n}
is a special case of a random code, it directly follows that
\mathcal{R}_{d}\subseteq\mathcal{R}_{r}
.

Lemma 10
.

Assume that
|\mathcal{S}|<\infty
.
If
\mathbf{Int}\left\{\mathcal{R}_{d}\right\}\neq\varnothing
then
\mathcal{R}_{d}\supseteq\mathcal{R}_{r}.

We prove this lemma in subsection
VIII-D

and it allows us to derandomize the code obtained in Theorem
3
.
That is, when
|\mathcal{S}|<\infty
,
the deterministic code capacity region
\mathcal{R}_{d}
is given by

\mathcal{R}^{\star}
defined in (
121
).

Next, we extend Lemma
10
to the case with a continuous set
\mathcal{S}
.
For this aim, we assume the following.

Assumption 2
.

The set
\mathcal{S}
is assumed to be a compact subset of

\mathbb{R}^{d}
. The map
s(\in\mathcal{S})\mapsto\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}

is assumed to be a
C^{1}
continuous map for
x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}
.
Then, the symmetric logarithmic derivative (SLD)
L_{x,y,s|j}
is defined as
[
61
, Section 6.2]

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(L_{x,y,s|j}\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}+\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}L_{x,y,s|j})=\frac{\partial}{\partial s^{j}}\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}.

(156)

The SLD Fisher information matrix
J_{x,y,s|j,j^{\prime}}
is defined as

\displaystyle J_{x,y,s|j,j^{\prime}}:=\mathsf{Tr\,}(\frac{\partial}{\partial s^{j}}\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}})L_{x,y,s|j^{\prime}}.

(157)

It is also assumed that the map
s(\in\mathcal{S})\mapsto J_{x,y,s|j,j^{\prime}}
is continuous.

We extend Lemma
10
as follows.

Lemma 11
.

Assume Assumption
2
.
If
\mathbf{Int}\left\{\mathcal{R}_{d}\right\}\neq\varnothing
then
\mathcal{R}_{d}\supseteq\mathcal{R}_{r}.

This lemma is shown in subsection
VIII-E
.
Combining Lemmas
9
and
11
, we have

Theorem
5
.

(
Deterministic Code Capacity Region
)
Assume Assumption
2
.
If
\mathbf{Int}\{\mathcal{R}_{d}\}\neq\varnothing
, then
\mathcal{R}_{d}=\mathcal{R}_{r}
.

Therefore, when Assumptions
1
and
2
hold,
the deterministic code capacity region
\mathcal{R}_{d}
is given by

\mathcal{R}^{\star}
defined in (
121
).

VIII-B

Proof of sufficiency in Lemma
8

We will show that if a CQ-AVMAC is either
\mathcal{X}-
symmetrizable or
\mathcal{Y}-
symmetrizable or
\mathcal{X}\mathcal{Y}-
symmetrizable then
\mathbf{Int}\{\mathcal{R}_{d}\}=\varnothing
.
In this part, we will not use Assumption
1
.

VIII-B
1
\mathcal{X}\mathcal{Y}-
symmetrizable case 1

We will first analyze the term (
158
) by assuming that the CQ-AVMAC is
\mathcal{X}\mathcal{Y}-
symmetrizable.
For simplicity, we assume that there exists a collection of distribution
\{U_{3}(.|x,y)\}_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}}
over
\mathcal{S}
that
achieves
0
in (
155
).
The error probability
e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})
can be written as follows,

\displaystyle e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})=\sum_{(\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2})\neq(m_{1},m_{2})}\mathrm{Tr}[D_{\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2}}\rho_{x^{n}(m_{1}),y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}].

(158)

We choose a random variable
S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2}}

subject to the probability measure
U_{3}^{n}(ds^{n}|x^{n}(\bar{m}_{1}),y^{n}(\bar{m}_{2}))
.
The expectation

\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2}}}\left[e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2}})\right]

with respect to the random variable
S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2}}

is calculated as follows.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2}}}\left[e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2}})\right]

\displaystyle{:=}\sum_{(\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2})\neq(m_{1},m_{2})}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{n}}U_{3}^{n}(ds^{n}|x^{n}(\bar{m}_{1}),y^{n}(\bar{m}_{2}))\mathrm{Tr}[D_{\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2}}\rho_{x^{n}(m_{1}),y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2})\neq(m_{1},m_{2})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[D_{\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{S}}U_{3}(ds_{i}|x_{i}(\bar{m}_{1}),y_{i}(\bar{m}_{2}))\rho_{x_{i}(m_{1}),y_{i}(m_{2}),s_{i}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{a}{=}\sum_{(\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2})\neq(m_{1},m_{2})}\mathrm{Tr}\left[D_{\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{S}}U_{3}(ds_{i}|x_{i}(m_{1}),y_{i}(m_{2}))\rho_{x_{i}(\bar{m}_{1}),y_{i}(\bar{m}_{2}),s_{i}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2})\neq(m_{1},m_{2})}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{n}}U_{3}^{n}(ds^{n}|x^{n}(m_{1}),y^{n}(m_{2}))\mathrm{Tr}[D_{\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2}}\rho_{x^{n}(\bar{m}_{1}),y^{n}(\bar{m}_{2}),s^{n}}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}],

(159)

where
a
follows from
\mathcal{X}\mathcal{Y}-
symmetrizabilty condition mentioned in (
155
). Similarly, we have

\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2}}}\left[e(\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},S^{n}_{m_{1},m_{2}})\right]=\sum_{(\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2})\neq(\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2})}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{n}}U_{3}^{n}(ds^{n}|x^{n}(m_{1}),y^{n}(m_{2}))\mathrm{Tr}[D_{\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2}}\rho_{x^{n}(\bar{m}_{1}),y^{n}(\bar{m}_{2}),s^{n}}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}].

(160)

We set
(\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2})\neq(m_{1},m_{2})
.
Since
any element
(\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2})\in\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}\times\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}
satisfies
the condition
(\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2})\neq({m}_{1},{m}_{2})

or
(\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2})\neq(\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2})
,
(
159
) and (
160
) imply
the relation

\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{m_{1},m_{2}}}\left[e(\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},S^{n}_{m_{1},m_{2}})\right]+\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2}}}\left[e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2}})\right]\geq 1.

(161)

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}||\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|}\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}=1}^{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|}\sum_{\hat{m}_{2}=1}^{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|}\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2}}}\left[\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},S_{\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2}}^{n})\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|^{2}|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|^{2}}\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}=1}^{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|}\sum_{\hat{m}_{2}=1}^{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|}\sum_{m_{1}=1}^{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|}\sum_{m_{2}=1}^{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|}\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2}}}\left[e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},S^{n}_{\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2}})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{b}{\geq}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|^{2}|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|^{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}||\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|\\
2\end{array}\right)=\frac{(|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}||\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|-1)}{2\cdot|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}||\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|}

\displaystyle\overset{c}{\geq}\frac{1}{4},

where
b
follows from the fact that
\left(\begin{array}[]{c}|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}||\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|\\
2\end{array}\right)

pairs
((\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2}),(m_{1},m_{2}))

satisfy the condition
(\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2})\neq(m_{1},m_{2})
, which implies
(
161
).

c
follows from the fact that
|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}||\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|\geq 2
since the cardinality of the smallest message set would be
2
(when the length of message is only
1
). Thus, it directly follows that there exists a message pair
({m}_{1},{m}_{2})\in[|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|]\times[|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|]
such that

\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{{m}_{1},{m}_{2}}}\left[\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},S_{{m}_{1},{m}_{2}}^{n})\right]\geq\frac{1}{4}.

Thus, the following holds

\sup_{s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\geq\frac{1}{4}.

(162)

Thus, if a CQ-AVMAC is
\mathcal{X}\mathcal{Y}
-symmetrizable then
\forall R_{1},R_{2}>0
, there does not exist an

(n,|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|,|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|,\varepsilon_{n})
deterministic code
\mathcal{C}_{n}
such that
\lim_{n\to\infty}\varepsilon_{n}=0.
This implies that
\mathbf{Int}\{\mathcal{R}_{d}\}=\varnothing
.

VIII-B
2
\mathcal{X}\mathcal{Y}-
symmetrizable case 2

Next, we consider the case when
the CQ-AVMAC is
\mathcal{X}\mathcal{Y}-
symmetrizable and
no collection of distribution
\{U_{3}(.|x,y)\}_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}}
over
\mathcal{S}

achieves
0
in (
155
).
In this case, there exists a sequence of
collections of distribution
\{U_{3,k}(.|x,y)\}_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}}
such that

\theta_{3,k}:=\max_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X},y,y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y},z\in\mathcal{Z}}\left\|\int_{\mathcal{S}}U_{3,k}(ds|x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\rho_{x,y,s}^{\mathcal{B}}-\int_{\mathcal{S}}U_{3,k}(ds|x,y)\rho_{x^{\prime},y^{\prime},s}^{\mathcal{B}}\right\|_{1}\to 0\hbox{ as }k\to\infty.

(163)

When we choose
\{U_{3,k}(.|x,y)\}_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}}

as
\{U_{3}(.|x,y)\}_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y}}

in the discussion of Subsubsection
VIII-B1
,
the difference between LHS and RHS in (
159
) is upper bounded by

n\theta_{3,k}
.
We choose a random variable
S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2}|k}

subject to the probability measure
U_{3,k}^{n}(ds^{n}|x^{n}(\bar{m}_{1}),y^{n}(\bar{m}_{2}))
.
Hence, instead of (
161
), we have

\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{m_{1},m_{2}|k}}\left[e(\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},S^{n}_{m_{1},m_{2}})\right]+\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2}|k}}\left[e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1},\bar{m}_{2}})\right]\geq 1-n\theta_{3,k}.

(164)

Thus,
there exists a message pair
({m}_{1},{m}_{2})\in[|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|]\times[|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|]
such that

\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{{m}_{1},{m}_{2}|k}}\left[\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},S_{{m}_{1},{m}_{2}}^{n})\right]\geq\frac{1}{4}(1-n\theta_{3,k}).

(165)

Combining (
163
) and (
165
), we obtain (
162
).

VIII-B
3
\mathcal{X}-
symmetrizable case

To discuss the case when
a CQ-AVMAC is
\mathcal{X}-
symmetrizable,
we rewrite the error probability
e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})
as follows

\displaystyle e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}\neq m_{1}}\mathrm{Tr}[D_{\hat{m}_{1},m_{2}}\rho_{x^{n}(m_{1}),y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}]}_{e_{1}(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})}+\underbrace{\sum_{\hat{m}_{2}\neq m_{2}}\mathrm{Tr}[D_{m_{1},\hat{m}_{2}}\rho_{x^{n}(m_{1}),y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}]}_{e_{2}(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})}+\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\hat{m}_{1}\neq m_{1}\\
\hat{m}_{2}\neq m_{2}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Tr}[D_{\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2}}\rho_{x^{n}(m_{1}),y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}]}_{e_{3}(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})}.

(166)

For simplicity, we assume that there exists a collection of distribution
\{U_{1}(.|x)\}_{x\in\mathcal{X}}
over
\mathcal{S}
that
achieves
0
in (
153
).
We choose a random variable
S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1}}

subject to the probability measure
U_{1}^{n}(ds^{n}|x^{n}(\bar{m}_{1}))
.
We employ the first term
e_{1}(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})
instead of
e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})
.
The expectation

\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1}}}\left[e_{1}(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1}})\right]

with respect to the random variable
S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1}}

is calculated as follows.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1}}}\left[e_{1}(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1}})\right]

\displaystyle{:=}\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}\neq m_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{n}}U_{1}^{n}(ds^{n}|x^{n}(\bar{m}_{1}))\mathrm{Tr}[D_{\hat{m}_{1},{m}_{2}}\rho_{x^{n}(m_{1}),y^{n}(m_{2}),s^{n}}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}\neq m_{1}}\mathrm{Tr}\left[D_{\hat{m}_{1},{m}_{2}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{S}}U_{1}(ds_{i}|x_{i}(\bar{m}_{1}))\rho_{x_{i}(m_{1}),y_{i}(m_{2}),s_{i}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{a}{=}\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}\neq m_{1}}\mathrm{Tr}\left[D_{\hat{m}_{1},{m}_{2}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\mathcal{S}}U_{1}(ds_{i}|x_{i}(m_{1}))\rho_{x_{i}(\bar{m}_{1}),y_{i}({m}_{2}),s_{i}}^{\mathcal{B}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}\neq m_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{n}}U_{1}^{n}(ds^{n}|x^{n}(m_{1}))\mathrm{Tr}[D_{\hat{m}_{1},{m}_{2}}\rho_{x^{n}(\bar{m}_{1}),y^{n}({m}_{2}),s^{n}}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}],

(167)

where
a
follows from
\mathcal{X}-
symmetrizabilty condition mentioned in (
153
). Similarly, we have

\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1}}}\left[e_{1}(\bar{m}_{1},{m}_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},S^{n}_{m_{1},m_{2}})\right]=\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}\neq\bar{m}_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{S}^{n}}U_{1}^{n}(ds^{n}|x^{n}(m_{1}))\mathrm{Tr}[D_{\hat{m}_{1},{m}_{2}}\rho_{x^{n}(\bar{m}_{1}),y^{n}({m}_{2}),s^{n}}^{\mathcal{B}^{n}}].

(168)

We choose
\bar{m}_{1}\neq m_{1}
.
Since any element

\hat{m}_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}
satisfies
the condition
\hat{m}_{1}\neq{m}_{1}

or
\hat{m}_{1}\neq\bar{m}_{1}
,
(
167
) and (
168
) imply
the relation

\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{m_{1}}}\left[e_{1}(\bar{m}_{1},{m}_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},S^{n}_{m_{1}})\right]+\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1}}}\left[e_{1}(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},S^{n}_{\bar{m}_{1}})\right]\geq 1.

(169)

Thus, we have,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|}\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}=1}^{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|}\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{\hat{m}_{1}}}\left[\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},S_{\hat{m}_{1}}^{n})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|^{2}|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|}\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}=1}^{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|}\sum_{m_{1}=1}^{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|}\sum_{m_{2}=1}^{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|}\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{\hat{m}_{1}}}\left[e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},S^{n}_{\hat{m}_{1}})\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|^{2}|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|}\sum_{\hat{m}_{1}=1}^{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|}\sum_{m_{1}=1}^{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|}\sum_{m_{2}=1}^{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|}\mathbb{E}_{S^{n}_{\hat{m}_{1}}}\left[e_{1}(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},S^{n}_{\hat{m}_{1}})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{b}{\geq}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|^{2}|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|\\
2\end{array}\right)\cdot|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{2}|=\frac{(|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|-1)}{2\cdot|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|}\overset{c}{\geq}\frac{1}{4},

where
b
follows from the fact that
\left(\begin{array}[]{c}|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|\\
2\end{array}\right)
pairs
(\bar{m}_{1},m_{1})

satisfy
\bar{m}_{1}\neq m_{1}
, which yields
(
169
).

c
follows from the fact that
|\mathcal{M}_{n}^{1}|\geq 2
since the cardinality of the smallest message set would be
2
. In the same way as the
\mathcal{X}\mathcal{Y}-
symmetrizable case, we have

\sup_{s^{n}\in\mathcal{S}^{n}}\bar{e}(\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})\geq\frac{1}{4}.

(170)

Further, when
no collection of distribution
\{U_{1}(.|x)\}_{x\in\mathcal{X}}
over
\mathcal{S}
achieves
0
in (
153
),
we can show (
170
) in the same way as subsubsection
VIII-B2
.

Using a similar discussion, we can derive
the property
\mathbf{Int}\{\mathcal{R}_{d}\}=\varnothing
.
When a CQ-AVMAC is
\mathcal{Y}-
symmetrizable,
using the second term
e_{2}(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n},s^{n})
,
we can derive the same property.
This completes the proof of sufficiency.

VIII-B
4
\mathcal{Y}-
symmetrizable case

When a CQ-AVMAC is
\mathcal{Y}-

)

.

VIII-C

Proof of necessity in Lemma
8

VIII-C
1
Classical channel

We will use a contrapositive argument, i.e., we will show that if a CQ-AVMAC is non-symmetrizable (i.e. none of the
eqs.

150
,
151
and
152
satisfies), then
\mathbf{Int}\{\mathcal{R}_{d}\}\neq\varnothing
.
For this proof, we employ the results in
[
20
]
for the classical case, which composed
their Lemma 1, Lemma 2, and Theorem 1.
Although the proof of Theorem 1 in
[
20
]

uses the finiteness of
\mathcal{S}
,
the proof of Lemma 1 in
[
20
]

does not use the finiteness of
\mathcal{S}
.
Lemma 1 in
[
20
]
is rewritten as follows.

We consider a classical arbitrary varying MAC (C-AVMAC)

\{W_{x,y,s}\}_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y},s\in\mathcal{S}}\subset\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{Z})

with
|\mathcal{X}|,|\mathcal{Y}|,|\mathcal{Z}|<\infty
.
We denote the set of possible empirical distributions on
\Omega

with
n
outcomes by
\mathcal{P}(n,\Omega)
.
We denote the typical subspace of
P\in\mathcal{P}(n,\Omega)

by
\mathcal{T}_{n}(P)
.

Definition 23

(Collection of subsets

\{D_{u,v|\kappa}\}_{(u,v)\in\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{V}}
)
.

We fix
P_{X}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{X})
,
P_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{Y})
,
Given small real numbers
\xi,\zeta,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}>0

and
\mathcal{U}\subset\mathcal{T}_{n}(P_{X}),\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{T}_{n}(P_{Y})
,
we simplify
\kappa:=(\xi,\zeta,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})
and
define the subsets
\{D_{u,v|\kappa}\subset\mathcal{Z}^{n}\}_{(u,v)\in\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{V}}
as follows.
The subset
D_{u,v|\kappa}
is defined as the set of elements
z^{n}\in\mathcal{Z}^{n}
that has a pair

(P_{XYSZ},s^{n})\in\mathcal{P}(n,\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\times\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{Z})\times\mathcal{S}^{n}

satisfying the following four conditions.

C0)

(u,v,s^{n},z^{n})\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(P_{XYSZ})
and

D(P_{XYSZ}\|P_{X}\times P_{Y}\times P_{S}\times P_{Z})\leq\xi
.

C1)

When a joint distribution
P_{XX^{\prime}YY^{\prime}SS^{\prime}Z}

and
u^{\prime}(\neq u)\in\mathcal{U},v^{\prime}(\neq v)\in\mathcal{V},s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}

satisfies the conditions

\displaystyle(u,u^{\prime},v,v^{\prime},s,s^{\prime},z^{n})\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(P_{XX^{\prime}YY^{\prime}SS^{\prime}Z})

(171)

\displaystyle D(P_{X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}S^{\prime}Z}\|P_{X^{\prime}}\times P_{Y^{\prime}}\times P_{S^{\prime}}\times P_{Z})\leq\xi,

(172)

we have

\displaystyle I(XYZ;X^{\prime}Y^{\prime}|S)<\zeta.

(173)

C2)

When a joint distribution
P_{XX^{\prime}YY^{\prime}SS^{\prime}Z}

and
u^{\prime}(\neq u)\in\mathcal{U},s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}

satisfies the conditions

\displaystyle(u,u^{\prime},v,s,s^{\prime},z^{n})\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(P_{XX^{\prime}YSS^{\prime}Z})

(174)

\displaystyle D(P_{X^{\prime}YS^{\prime}Z}\|P_{X^{\prime}}\times P_{Y}\times P_{S^{\prime}}\times P_{Z})\leq\xi,

(175)

we have

\displaystyle I(XYZ;X^{\prime}Y|S)<\zeta_{1}.

(176)

C3)

When a joint distribution
P_{XYY^{\prime}SS^{\prime}Z}

and
v^{\prime}(\neq v)\in\mathcal{V},s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}

satisfies the conditions

\displaystyle(u,v,v^{\prime},s,s^{\prime},z^{n})\in\mathcal{T}_{n}(P_{XYY^{\prime}SS^{\prime}Z})

(177)

\displaystyle D(P_{XY^{\prime}S^{\prime}Z}\|P_{X}\times P_{Y^{\prime}}\times P_{S^{\prime}}\times P_{Z})\leq\xi,

(178)

we have

\displaystyle I(XYZ;Y^{\prime}|S)<\zeta_{2}.

(179)

Then Reference
[
20
]
showed the following.

Proposition 1

(
[
20
, Lemma 1]
)
.

Assume that an AVMAC

\{W_{x,y,s}\}_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y},s\in\mathcal{S}}
with
|\mathcal{X}|,|\mathcal{Y}|,|\mathcal{Z}|<\infty

is non-symmetrizable.
We fix
\alpha,\beta>0
.
We choose positive numbers
\kappa=(\xi,\zeta,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})

to satisfy the following conditions
1
1

1

Lemma 1 of
[
20
]
gives the concrete choice of

\kappa=(\xi,\zeta,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})
gives in its proof.
In their choice of
\kappa
,
a constant

is used to express

Since this coefficient is known to
\sqrt{2}

in (

).

\displaystyle\xi+\zeta_{1}<\frac{1}{8}\alpha^{2}\beta^{2}\theta_{1}^{2},\quad\xi+\zeta_{2}<\frac{1}{8}\alpha^{2}\beta^{2}\theta_{2}^{2},\quad\xi+\zeta<\frac{1}{64}|\mathcal{X}|^{-2}|\mathcal{Y}|^{-2}|\mathcal{Z}|^{-2}\alpha^{4}\beta^{4}\theta^{2},

where

\theta
are defined in
Definition
22
.
When
P_{X}\in\mathcal{P}(n,\mathcal{X})
,

That is, it showed the following;
When the collection of subsets

are not disjoint,

180
) does not hold.

Hence, when
\kappa
satisfies the condition of Proposition
1

and two subsets

we have the tuple
\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V},\{D_{u,v|\kappa}\}_{(u,v)\in\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{V}}
forms a code.
This code is written as

\mathcal{C}_{n}[\kappa,\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V}]
.
We also define

\displaystyle\gamma(n,|\mathcal{X}|,|\mathcal{Y}|,|\mathcal{S}|,|\mathcal{Z}|):=(n+1)^{|\mathcal{X}|^{2}|\mathcal{Y}|^{2}|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{Z}|}.

(181)

Then,
the combination of Lemma 2 and Theorem 1 of
[
20
]

is rewritten as follows.

Proposition 2

(
[
20
, Lemma 2 & Theorem 1]
)
.

Assume that an AVMAC

\{W_{x,y,s}\}_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y},s\in\mathcal{S}}

,

\kappa=(\xi,\zeta,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M_{r}^{2}}\sum_{m_{1}=1}^{M_{r}}\sum_{m_{2}=1}^{M_{r}}e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n}[\kappa,\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V}],s^{n})

for

When we choose

\displaystyle\log(1+\varepsilon)<-\max(-\xi/2,8r+2\varepsilon^{\prime}-\zeta,4r+2\varepsilon^{\prime}-\zeta_{1},4r+2\varepsilon^{\prime}-\zeta_{2}),

using the property (
2
), we can rewrite

Lemma 12
.

Assume that an AVMAC

\{W_{x,y,s}\}_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y},s\in\mathcal{S}}

1
.

\alpha,\beta>0
,

\kappa=(\xi,\zeta,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})

.
We choose

\varepsilon^{\prime},\varepsilon,\delta,r>0
,
a subset
\mathcal{S}_{\varepsilon}\subset\mathcal{S}
,

) and (

), (

), and

), respectively.

two subsets
\mathcal{U}\subset\mathcal{T}_{n}(P_{X}),\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{T}_{n}(P_{Y})

|\mathcal{U}|=|\mathcal{V}|=m_{r}:=e^{nr}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{M_{r}^{2}}\sum_{m_{1}=1}^{M_{r}}\sum_{m_{2}=1}^{M_{r}}e(m_{1},m_{2},\mathcal{C}_{n}[\kappa,\mathcal{U},\mathcal{V}],s^{n})

for

.

) goes to zero exponentially,

Lemma 13
.

Assume that an AVMAC

\{W_{x,y,s}\}_{x\in\mathcal{X},y\in\mathcal{Y},s\in\mathcal{S}}

1
and

\mathbf{Int}\{\mathcal{R}_{d}\}\neq\varnothing
holds for
